The principal target is fixed; all source motion is exactly ∂gD± . The logarithm supplies the connected subtractions. Theorem 10.4 (Endpoint packet-shell supplier). For the graph-segment family constructed above, the complete twice-marked endpoint expansion has the following properties. (i) The grounded periodic covariance has volume-independent AA, AF, and FF rows of orders two, three, and four. After the exact global contact is subtracted, every complete reflection/BRS packet has two lattice-difference factors and therefore gains (r/ℓi)2 at weak scale i. (ii) The exact weak recurrence admits a finite crossing-cell decomposition. On each fixed cell, both g derivatives are taken before the labelled large-field partition and before bad-core ownership. The differentiated partition is then recollected with the same charged events and with no same-owner cancellation assumption. (iii) Before crossing, the full rooted stable map, including wrapping and charged-flat coordinates, is C2 in g with a strictly contracting same-order marked row. At first crossing, the fresh marked family lies in a uniform hybrid pivot–jet polydisc and satisfies the original-link Fourier–Wiener incidence row required by the joint Haar–optical collect. (iv) The crossing contribution and the no-crossing terminal contribution are controlled by one geometri- cally summable conditional-packet shell majorant. The same majorant controls the functional and its first two g derivatives, extends to the closed crossing cells, and sends the terminal remainder to zero uniformly as the terminal depth tends to infinity. Consequently the complete normalized two-root functional, with or without one fixed dimension-six defect root, has a cutoff-, volume-, crossing-cell-, and terminal-depth-independent C2 bound on 0 ≤ g ≤ gr. Proof. Appendix A gives the full proof inside this manuscript. Its five subsections establish the grounded- torus covariance and globally subtracted packet division; the weak crossing recurrence and wrapping rows; the twice-marked conditional-packet contraction; the pre-owner hybrid-polydisc Wiener and compact- entrance estimates; and the terminal-shell summation. No additional mathematical input is imported beyond the results proved in Sections 3–7. Scale-uniform pullback defect row. Uniformly for 0 ≤ g ≤ gr, ∥D± g,r∥r,[1] ≤ Cf r2 L(r)pf . (181) The same row holds after reflection and with every finite passive source mark used in the two-root stability estimate. sup 0≤g≤gr ∥D± g,r∥r,[1] ≤ Cf r2 L(r)pf . (182) The marked first-exit theorem and complementary high-jet source transport both begin two engineering degrees above the fixed principal target. Their scale-normalized rows are therefore bounded by Cf r2 L(r)pf uniformly on the common graph segment. This estimate uses no coupling derivative and so requires only the already proved finite marked source bridge. 10.3 Dimension-six source comparison and noncollapse The exact source decomposition is O± g,r = b O± r + D± g,r, (183) with ∥D± g,r∥[1] ≤ Cf r2 L(r)pf . (184) 92 Two-root stability under the dimension-six defect. Let b Qr(g) be the reflected connected form formed with the fixed principal sources. Then |Qr(g) − b Qr(g)| ≤ Cf r2 L(r)pf . (185) Expand the connected two-root form using O± = b O± + D± . Every difference term contains a defect root and is bounded by the two-root version of Theorem 10.2; the two-defect term is smaller. Compatibility of the physical scale with the common diagonal. Let f be the profile of Lemma 10.1, and let the constants Cf , pf be those in the uniform endpoint and source-defect rows above. Choose the fixed countable source-scale set used in Corollary 8.12 to contain a cofinal dyadic sequence rn ↓ 0. There is an index n such that, for r = rn, Cf  u(r) + r2 L(r)pf  ≤ 1 2 V0(f). Before taking the common diagonal subsequence, enlarge the countable source algebra by the fixed fields Yr(f), ΘYr(f), and their reflected product. The same single diagonal extraction then gives all Schwinger limits entering their OS form and preserves every cutoff- and volume-uniform lower bound. The scale r is not changed after extraction. The cofinal dyadic choice is countable, while u(r) → 0 and r2 L(r)pf → 0 along it. Hence one r satisfies the displayed inequality. Adding finitely many fields at that scale preserves countability of the source algebra. The source-tree theorem and Corollary 8.12 therefore permit the same diagonal argument, and convergence of the finitely many Schwinger functions passes a uniform finite-cutoff lower bound to the limit. Theorem 10.5 (Interacting-to-Gaussian reflected two-root comparison). For the profile of Lemma 10.1, uniformly in cutoff, volume, and preceding ultraviolet depth, |Sur (ΘYr(f)Yr(f)) − V0(f)| ≤ Cf  u(r) + r2 L(r)pf  . (186) Consequently, for sufficiently small fixed dyadic r, ∥[Yr(f)]∥2 OS ≥ 1 2 V0(f) > 0. (187) Proof. The master endpoint theorem, equivalently Theorem 10.2, and evenness give | b Qr(gr) − b Qr(0)| ≤ Z gr 0 Cf g dg ≤ Cf ur. At g = 0 the law is the grounded tangent Gaussian and the principal source is the Gaussian Wick source, so b Qr(0) = V0(f). Add the dimension-six source-defect estimate below; its source error is Cf r2 L(r)pf . Choose r and the common subsequence by the compatibility argument above; the total error is then at most V0(f)/2. Reflection-positive recombination identifies the finite-cutoff form with the squared OS norm, and the same lemma passes the lower bound to the common limit. 10.4 Limiting curvature vector and finite spectral window Lemma 10.6 (Survival of the reflected curvature vector in the common limit). Let Yr(f) be a fixed positive-time reflected local curvature functional. Suppose along the common diagonal subsequence: (i) every Schwinger function entering Sa,L(ΘYr(f)Yr(f)) converges; (ii) reflection-positive recombination has already been performed; (iii) uniformly in cutoff and volume, Sa,L(ΘYr(f)Yr(f)) ≥ 1 2 V0(f) > 0. Then the limiting Osterwalder–Schrader vector ψY = [Yr(f)] is nonzero and satisfies ∥ψY ∥2 OS = lim Sa,L(ΘYr(f)Yr(f)) ≥ 1 2 V0(f) > 0. 93 Proof. The OS norm of this fixed local polynomial is a finite linear combination of the convergent limiting Schwinger functions. Therefore its finite-cutoff values converge to the limiting quadratic form. The uniform positive lower bound passes to the limit. Corollary 10.7 (Finite positive local spectral window without Wilson pushforward). Let ψY be the nonzero limiting curvature vector from Lemma 10.6. If common physical clustering and the OS spectral argument give supp µY ⊂ [m⋆, ∞), m⋆ > 0, then there exists a finite MY ≥ m⋆ such that µY ([m⋆, MY ]) > 0. No exact Wilson pushforward, Wilson moment identity, or reducing retained Hilbert subspace is used. Proof. The finite spectral measure has total mass 0 < µY ([m⋆, ∞)) = ∥ψY ∥2 OS < ∞. Since [m⋆, n] ↑ [m⋆, ∞), continuity from below implies that some finite n has positive mass. Set MY = n. 11 Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups Theorem 11.1 (Completion of the fixed-group construction). For every fixed compact connected simple G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds. Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors with Ig, so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus, with the universal color factor CA(G). The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG, and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam, reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction, and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG, the first proof call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is color-free, tensors with Ig, or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space. Corollary 11.2 (The SU(N) family). For every fixed integer N ≥ 2, the theorem holds for G = SU(N) in the defining representation, and the scale-two map is u(2L) = u(L) + 11N log 2 24π2 u(L)2 + ON (u(L)3 ). No estimate uniform as N → ∞ is asserted. Remark 11.3 (Logical scope). The construction is a fixed-group theorem. The directed subsequence, curvature-jet observable scope, and absence of a large-rank uniformity claim are part of the statement, not omitted conclusions. A Endpoint packet-shell verification This appendix supplies the detailed proof of Theorem 10.4. It is part of the same manuscript and uses the standing uniformity convention of Section 1. The main text isolates the logical endpoint theorem; the statements below retain the full finite-volume, crossing-cell, pre-owner, compact-entrance, and terminal-shell verification needed for that theorem. 94 A.1 Grounded covariance, contact subtraction, and weak admission Lemma A.1 (Grounded-torus curvature covariance). Let C (L) r be the zero-mode-removed transverse covariance remaining after the weak flow is stopped at scale r on a periodic torus of side L ≥ 4r. For j = 0, 1, 2, |∇j C(L) r (x, y)| ≤ Cjr−2−j (1 + dL(x, y)/r)−(2+j) , with constants independent of L. The corresponding AA, AF, and FF rows have decay orders two, three, and four. Proof. Write C (L) r = R ∞ r2 (e−t∆L − Π0)ΠT dt. For t ≤ L2 , periodization gives |K (L) t (x, y) − L−4 | ≤ Ct−2 e−cdL(x,y)2 /t + CL−4 , and derivative rows without the constant term. For t ≥ L2 , use the nonzero spectral gap. The transverse projector commutes with the heat semigroup and has the standard scalar-plus-two-derivative representation. Integration gives the stated rows; the scalar L−4 contribution is O(L−2 ) and is absorbed because dL ≤ 2L. Definition A.2 (Globally subtracted even packet kernels). Let Tab µν(p) be the complete two-open-leg coefficient of the normalized generating functional after all longitudinal, ghost, determinant, normalization, and source contacts have been included. The background Ward identity identifies its exact local contact T(0). Define Tren (p) = T(p) − T(0) before any absolute value is taken. A packet selector is a scalar, color-blind, translation-covariant and reflection-invariant function of the exact conditional scale labels and reported support geometry. Apply such a selector only after the whole BRS/reflection packet and its normalization contacts have been collected. If T = P λ Tλ is the resulting disjoint packet decomposition, absolute convergence gives the exact allocation Tren (p) = X λ Tλ(p) − Tλ(0)  . No cut through an individual packet and no shellwise Ward identity is used. Lemma A.3 (Exact even-contact division of complete packets). For every complete packet class λ, the one-open-leg coefficient vanishes by global color invariance. The renormalized two-open-leg packet is reflection even, vanishes at p = 0, and admits the exact second-order division Tλ(p) − Tλ(0) = X α,β b pα b pβSλ,αβ(p). (188) For every 0 < a′ < a0, the rooted norm of Sλ is bounded by the second support moment of the same complete packet at exponent a0. If the complete packet is represented at weak scale i, the two displayed difference factors carry the canonical relative factor (r/ℓi)2 = 2−2i (189) with respect to the fixed physical source scale r. This factor belongs to the whole packet. It is not assigned to a chosen branch or attachment cut. Proof. The selector is inserted only after the exact normalized packet is formed and is scalar under color, translation and aligned reflection. Hence every selected sum is again a complete packet. Its one-open-leg coefficient is an invariant adjoint vector and therefore vanishes. Global contact subtraction is allocated after the disjoint packet decomposition, so each selected renormalized coefficient vanishes at p = 0. Reflection gives Tλ(−p) = Tλ(p) and therefore removes the linear Taylor term. The integral Taylor formula, or equivalently finite Laurent telescoping in the variables eipα , gives (188). Two momentum derivatives insert two support displacements, absorbed by the unused exponent a0 − a′ . The divided packet has no reflection-even scalar or rank-two local component of engineering dimension four or five. Lemmas 5.22 and 5.23 therefore place its local projection in the stable degree-six block; this observation will be used only for stable membership, not to estimate the nonlocal packet. The scale factor in (189) is instead purely geometric. In the tangent-normalized profile coordinates, one lattice difference at representation scale ℓi = 2i r is (r/ℓi) times the corresponding unit-scale difference. The two explicit factors in (188) therefore contribute exactly (r/ℓi)2 = 2−2i . The two support moments of Sλ are absorbed by the unused rooted exponent. This argument applies to the whole packet, local and nonlocal together. Since the division identity holds for the entire C2 family, zero, one or two g derivatives do not remove the two explicit differences. 95 Lemma A.4 (g-marked one-scale admission). There is g0 > 0 such that, for real 0 ≤ g ≤ g0, the complete normalized interaction and all declared source/defect marks are twice continuously differentiable in the permanent regulator space. An undistinguished non-Gaussian child has parent-normalized row η0(g) ≤ Cg, while a child carrying one or two distinguished g derivatives has row η1(g) + η2(g) ≤ C. The exactly recombined large-field/irregular row and its first two g derivatives extend continuously by zero at g = 0 and are bounded by Cmg−pm e−c/g2 for m ≤ 2. No claim that an attached odd child is O(g2 ) is made. Proof. In the scale-normalized Gaussian coordinate, the cubic Yang–Mills species starts at order g, the quartic and other non-Gaussian even species start at order g2 , and the common species tube controls all field and source marks. Thus an unmarked species row is Cg, while one or two explicit g derivatives remove at most the corresponding powers and give a finite row. The regulator allocations are additive under multiplication, and the Gram and Pruefer estimates in the summed replica–BKAR theorem are insensitive to the parity of an attached tensor. The ordinary parent-normalized child recursion therefore closes for Cg0 below its printed threshold, with two fixed derivative marks handled by Leibniz. The C3 -flat irregular estimate in u = g2 gives the displayed first and second g-derivative rows after the chain rule. In particular the model child gA3 differentiated once by a covariance edge is correctly admitted as an O(g) child, and one g derivative of that child is O(1). Lemma A.5 (Cellwise complete shell decomposition). Fix the finite directed depth M and one crossing cell, including the no-crossing cell. After the selected exact factorization has been completed, apply the canonical reported-support selector only to whole normalized packets. The separated part of the two-root functional has an exact disjoint decomposition b Qsep r,M (g) = X k≥0 FM,k(g) (190) whose coefficients are C2 on the relative interior of that cell. Every normalization, denominator, localiza- tion, irregular and source-motion contact belongs to exactly one selected packet. No assertion that the individual coefficients FM,k agree for two different stopping factorizations is needed or made. Proof. At fixed finite volume and depth, the reporting-telescope and owner-map identities form a disjoint finite or absolutely convergent inventory of complete normalized packets. Their canonical physical reported supports therefore admit disjoint scalar dyadic selectors after packet completion. Inside one crossing cell the stopping depth and exact factorization are fixed, so the graph-segment C2 identity and the finite marked forest permit termwise differentiation. Summing the selectors gives the unselected exact functional, proving (190). Definition A.6 (Finite directed continuation and crossing cells). Fix the physical weak scale r, its endpoint gr = √ ur, and an arbitrary finite further directed depth M. Choose one terminal coarse side Mout and set Mi = 2M−i Mout. The side is first chosen admissible for Lemma 6.1 and is enlarged once, independently of M, to satisfy the fixed-torus smallness threshold of Theorem A.9. For every 0 ≤ g ≤ gr, use the exact weak recurrence u0(g) = g2 , ui+1(g) = Fi(ui(g)), gi(g) = p ui(g), 0 ≤ i < M. Let NM (g) := min{0 ≤ i ≤ M : ui(g) ≥ ū}, with NM (g) = ∞ if the set is empty. Uniform outgoing transversality implies that, when NM (g) < ∞, ui(g) < ū (i < NM (g)), uNM (g)(g) ∈ I⋆. The relative interiors of the sets on which NM (g) = N are called the crossing cells. On such a cell the exact factorization consists of N weak conditionals followed by the printed finite macrobridge and joint compact–optical logarithm. On the no-crossing cell it consists of all M weak conditionals followed by the terminal weak logarithm on the fixed coarse torus. 96 This is a choice of exact factorization, not a modification of the measure. The integer NM (g) is never differentiated. Derivatives are taken inside one crossing cell, where the depth is fixed, and are extended to cell boundaries using the original finite-volume C2 normalized functional. At g = 0 one always uses the no-crossing factorization and later sends M → ∞. Definition A.7 (Canonical finite-volume off-graph coordinates). For the directed schedule in Defini- tion A.6, put εi := e−σMi , δi := δG(Mi), Mi = 2M−i Mout. The first quantity records genuine wrapping histories in the interaction; the second records the scalar massless finite-size comparison. After each complete connected output has been formed and assigned its canonical reported hull, let Pwr i select precisely the history certificates containing at least one periodic wrapping occurrence at some scale not later than i. If Ri is the exact finite-volume centered interaction and R⋆(ui) is the rooted-thermodynamic invariant graph, set Ωi := Ri − R⋆(ui), Wi := Pwr i Ωi, Bi := (I − Pwr i )Ωi. (191) Thus Ωi = Wi + Bi coefficientwise. Both Ri and R⋆(ui) are written in the same exact schedule-fixed vacuum and tangent-normalized coupling coordinates. The coordinate intertwining and supportwise extraction identity therefore give E [2] X Ωi,X = 0 for every reported support X; all three off-graph coordinates lie in the actual centered stable fiber. The first coordinate is the canonical sum of histories carrying a wrapping certificate. After the tangent-normalized scalar coordinate is extracted, the massless acoustic sum–integral correction belongs to the nonautonomous scalar map Fi and is not an off-graph activity. By the exact regular interaction comparison and label recombination, the only nonwrapping off-graph difference is the slaved U4 charged/atlas complement; this is Bi and is exponentially flat in ui. No flat charged coefficient is identified with a wrapping history. A.2 Weak recurrence, wrapping control, and crossing cells Lemma A.8 (Full rooted finite-torus stable-map comparison). (196) All constants are uniform in cutoff, original volume and finite directed depth. Proof. Use the complete labelled connected-history representation before forgetting owners. In the scheduled torus and in the rooted thermodynamic step, pair a history whenever its canonically reported hull has an injective periodic lift and contains no charged atlas label. The local Karcher, Gaussian, coarea, determinant, localization, extraction and collect templates are then literally the same, so paired histories and their derivatives cancel coefficientwise. An unpaired regular history has a nontrivial periodic cycle; its reported hull has length at least cMi. Weakening the permanent rooted support exponent once gives Ce−σ0Mi . An unpaired charged history belongs to the exactly recombined U4 row and is Cu−P e−c0/u . For (193), insert H as one distinguished input atom before the connected logarithm. The permanent one-step derivative envelope is a rooted absolute sum with that atom retained once. The same periodic-lift pairing applies, and the long hull still pays e−σ0Mi ; no derivative can shorten the already assigned reported hull. Vacuum, two-jet and coupling extraction are finite-range linear maps and are paired in the same way. 97 Passing to the adapted norm is harmless: that norm is a fixed finite sum of rooted norms of powers of the rooted-thermodynamic linear map, and each power preserves the preceding paired/unpaired classification. At R = R⋆(u), the free term cancels because both maps are written in the same schedule-fixed vacuum coordinates. The stable tangent uH1 is exponentially local but need not have finite support, so an unpaired periodic image may already be of order εiu. This sharp row gives (192); no quadratic stable cancellation is asserted. If the output is projected to the wrapping ideal, a charged history without a prior periodic certificate must also acquire a new nontrivial periodic cycle. It therefore pays both the charged flat row and εi+1. The same observation applied to a nonwrapping distinguished input proves (194) and (195). These are statements about the full centered interaction map, not inferences from the scalar comparison. Finally differentiate the labelled identities before summing histories. A g mark either stays on the distinguished input or hits one of finitely many local atoms, the scalar orbit, the extraction projector, or a new periodic connector. In the first case the same pairing proves the same-order coefficient in the statement. In every other case the marked child row and the trajectory ledger give the polynomial factor, while the periodic hull or charged event remains present. This proves (196) through two marks. A.3 Twice-marked conditional packets and even-contact contraction Lemma A.10 (Cellwise half-power and two-mark ledger). There are constants Cm and finite exponents pm such that, uniformly in M, in the crossing cell, and in every index before the selected terminal factor, i X h=0 uh(g) ≤ C log(i + 2), i X h=0 |gh(g)| ≤ C √ i + 1, (206) |∂m g ui(g)| + |∂m g gi(g)| ≤ Cm(i + 2)pm , m = 0, 1, 2. (207) The same estimates hold on the no-crossing cell through i = M. Proof. On a cell with crossing depth N, the reciprocal comparison from Theorem 6.4 gives ui(g) ≤ C(N − i + A)−1 before the crossing. On the no-crossing cell the direct fixed-schedule estimate (201) gives the same row with N replaced by M; no extension of the nonautonomous schedule is made. Summation gives (206). 99 For crossing cells use the printed marked C3 orbit row. For the no-crossing cell use (199). In either case every Fi fixes zero, has positive derivative, and can be written as Fi(u) = uϕi(u) with ϕi positive and uniformly C2 . Its finite composition therefore factors as ui(g) = g2 ψi(g2 ) with ψi > 0. Differentiating the exact recurrence twice and using F′ i (ui) ≤ 1 + Cui and |F′′ i (ui)| ≤ C gives polynomial growth in i. Consequently gi(g) = g p ψi(g2) is an odd C2 function and no 1/g singularity occurs at the Gaussian endpoint. The interaction marks required by the finite conditional forest are simultaneously controlled by (203) and (204). All constants are uniform on the compact first-crossing interval and on the enlarged no-crossing weak wedge. Lemma A.11 (Exact finite-depth twice-marked conditional forest). At finite directed depth M, differen- tiate the exact factorization of Definition A.6 with respect to the two curvature roots and at most two copies of g. The result is a finite sum of complete scale-labelled packet trees such that: (i) the two source leaves have one unique lowest common ancestor; (ii) each g mark is owned exactly once by a trajectory coordinate, a conditional logarithm, a source return, the terminal weak logarithm, or the compact handoff logarithm; (iii) every covariance edge, hard-core derivative, field factorial, regulator allocation and normalization contact belongs to one complete one-step packet before multiscale composition; (iv) the coefficients are exactly those of the Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula. No infinite-depth passage is asserted in this lemma. Proof. Apply the joint-cumulant derivative identity to every exact normalized weak conditional and, on a crossing cell, to the exact finite macrobridge and joint compact–optical logarithm. Then apply the Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula to the finite composition. This assertion is coefficientwise and uses only finite-volume differentiability; the uniform compact marked bound is supplied later by Theorem A.20. The one-step summed replica–BKAR theorem controls each weak packet, Lemma A.4 controls zero, one and two g marks, and finite source transport controls the external roots. Uniqueness of the lowest common ancestor is the ordinary tree property. Lemma A.12 (Top marked ancestor and complete-packet shell). In a packet tree from Lemma A.11, contract every maximal descendant subtree carrying no distinguished label into its exact parent-normalized decoration, and suppress every maximal unary chain into its exact ordered transfer product. The distin- guished labels are the two curvature roots and the zero, one or two active g marks. They have a unique lowest common ancestor v⊤; let j be its conditional scale. Call v⊤ the top marked ancestor. All vertices strictly above v⊤ are unmarked. The complete coefficient created at v⊤ includes every contracted side decoration with its canonical reported support. Decompose these whole coefficients by their full reported-support shell k using a scalar translation- and reflection-invariant selector. The selected class is therefore a complete BRS/reflection packet with every denominator and normalization contact still inside it. If j ≤ k + C0, the permanent rooted-support row of that whole packet gives C(1 + 2k−j )q e−c2k−j . (208) If j > k + C0, the full support lies inside a fixed enlargement of one scale-j cell and no spatial connector estimate is needed. Proof. Contraction of an unlabelled descendant is its exact conditional value, so it retains its canonical reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the parity transfer product. The unique lowest common ancestor of all labels is an ordinary tree invariant. If a g derivative lands on a coarser conditional map, its mark is attached at that map and therefore moves v⊤ to that scale. Hence no marked vertex can occur above v⊤. The shell selector is applied only after the full packet at v⊤ has been collected. A remote unmarked side decoration is not cut from its parent and is not asked to carry an external Taylor factor. If exact localization turns its contribution into a smaller-hull contact, that term is already in the finite contact block and is not a shell-k packet. Otherwise its canonical reported support remains part of the whole packet. When that support spans 2k−j scale-j blocks, the strict rooted-support norm and the weak connector theorem give (208), including covariance, hard-core and mixed alternatives. The exact Loc/collect identity of Theorem 5.8 ensures that this dichotomy is made before the stable packet is declared. 100 Theorem A.13 (Complete rooted even-contact contraction). Set MY = n. 11 Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups Theorem 11.1 (Completion of the fixed-group construction). For every fixed compact connected simple G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds. Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors with Ig, so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus, with the universal color factor CA(G). The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG, and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam, reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction, and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG, the first proof call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is color-free, tensors with Ig, or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space. For every fixed integer N ≥ 2, the theorem holds for G = SU(N) in the defining representation, and the scale-two map is u(2L) = u(L) + 11N log 2 24π2 u(L)2 + ON (u(L)3 ). No estimate uniform as N → ∞ is asserted. Remark 11.3 (Logical scope). The construction is a fixed-group theorem. A Endpoint packet-shell verification This appendix supplies the detailed proof of Theorem 10.4. It is part of the same manuscript and uses the standing uniformity convention of Section 1. The main text isolates the logical endpoint theorem; the statements below retain the full finite-volume, crossing-cell, pre-owner, compact-entrance, and terminal-shell verification needed for that theorem. 94 A.1 Grounded covariance, contact subtraction, and weak admission Lemma A.1 (Grounded-torus curvature covariance). Let C (L) r be the zero-mode-removed transverse covariance remaining after the weak flow is stopped at scale r on a periodic torus of side L ≥ 4r. For j = 0, 1, 2, |∇j C(L) r (x, y)| ≤ Cjr−2−j (1 + dL(x, y)/r)−(2+j) , with constants independent of L. The corresponding AA, AF, and FF rows have decay orders two, three, and four. Proof. Write C (L) r = R ∞ r2 (e−t∆L − Π0)ΠT dt. For t ≤ L2 , periodization gives |K (L) t (x, y) − L−4 | ≤ Ct−2 e−cdL(x,y)2 /t + CL−4 , and derivative rows without the constant term. For t ≥ L2 , use the nonzero spectral gap. The transverse projector commutes with the heat semigroup and has the standard scalar-plus-two-derivative representation. Integration gives the stated rows; the scalar L−4 contribution is O(L−2 ) and is absorbed because dL ≤ 2L. Definition A.2 (Globally subtracted even packet kernels). Let Tab µν(p) be the complete two-open-leg coefficient of the normalized generating functional after all longitudinal, ghost, determinant, normalization, and source contacts have been included. The background Ward identity identifies its exact local contact T(0). Define Tren (p) = T(p) − T(0) before any absolute value is taken. A packet selector is a scalar, color-blind, translation-covariant and reflection-invariant function of the exact conditional scale labels and reported support geometry. Apply such a selector only after the whole BRS/reflection packet and its normalization contacts have been collected. If T = P λ Tλ is the resulting disjoint packet decomposition, absolute convergence gives the exact allocation Tren (p) = X λ Tλ(p) − Tλ(0)  . No cut through an individual packet and no shellwise Ward identity is used. Lemma A.3 (Exact even-contact division of complete packets). For every complete packet class λ, the one-open-leg coefficient vanishes by global color invariance. The renormalized two-open-leg packet is reflection even, vanishes at p = 0, and admits the exact second-order division Tλ(p) − Tλ(0) = X α,β b pα b pβSλ,αβ(p). (188) For every 0 < a′ < a0, the rooted norm of Sλ is bounded by the second support moment of the same complete packet at exponent a0. If the complete packet is represented at weak scale i, the two displayed difference factors carry the canonical relative factor (r/ℓi)2 = 2−2i (189) with respect to the fixed physical source scale r. This factor belongs to the whole packet. It is not assigned to a chosen branch or attachment cut. Proof. The selector is inserted only after the exact normalized packet is formed and is scalar under color, translation and aligned reflection. Hence every selected sum is again a complete packet. Its one-open-leg coefficient is an invariant adjoint vector and therefore vanishes. Global contact subtraction is allocated after the disjoint packet decomposition, so each selected renormalized coefficient vanishes at p = 0. Reflection gives Tλ(−p) = Tλ(p) and therefore removes the linear Taylor term. The integral Taylor formula, or equivalently finite Laurent telescoping in the variables eipα , gives (188). Two momentum derivatives insert two support displacements, absorbed by the unused exponent a0 − a′ . The divided packet has no reflection-even scalar or rank-two local component of engineering dimension four or five. Lemmas 5.22 and 5.23 therefore place its local projection in the stable degree-six block; this observation will be used only for stable membership, not to estimate the nonlocal packet. The scale factor in (189) is instead purely geometric. In the tangent-normalized profile coordinates, one lattice difference at representation scale ℓi = 2i r is (r/ℓi) times the corresponding unit-scale difference. The two explicit factors in (188) therefore contribute exactly (r/ℓi)2 = 2−2i . The two support moments of Sλ are absorbed by the unused rooted exponent. This argument applies to the whole packet, local and nonlocal together. Since the division identity holds for the entire C2 family, zero, one or two g derivatives do not remove the two explicit differences. 95 Lemma A.4 (g-marked one-scale admission). There is g0 > 0 such that, for real 0 ≤ g ≤ g0, the complete normalized interaction and all declared source/defect marks are twice continuously differentiable in the permanent regulator space. An undistinguished non-Gaussian child has parent-normalized row η0(g) ≤ Cg, while a child carrying one or two distinguished g derivatives has row η1(g) + η2(g) ≤ C. The exactly recombined large-field/irregular row and its first two g derivatives extend continuously by zero at g = 0 and are bounded by Cmg−pm e−c/g2 for m ≤ 2. No claim that an attached odd child is O(g2 ) is made. Proof. In the scale-normalized Gaussian coordinate, the cubic Yang–Mills species starts at order g, the quartic and other non-Gaussian even species start at order g2 , and the common species tube controls all field and source marks. Thus an unmarked species row is Cg, while one or two explicit g derivatives remove at most the corresponding powers and give a finite row. The regulator allocations are additive under multiplication, and the Gram and Pruefer estimates in the summed replica–BKAR theorem are insensitive to the parity of an attached tensor. The ordinary parent-normalized child recursion therefore closes for Cg0 below its printed threshold, with two fixed derivative marks handled by Leibniz. The C3 -flat irregular estimate in u = g2 gives the displayed first and second g-derivative rows after the chain rule. In particular the model child gA3 differentiated once by a covariance edge is correctly admitted as an O(g) child, and one g derivative of that child is O(1). Lemma A.5 (Cellwise complete shell decomposition). Fix the finite directed depth M and one crossing cell, including the no-crossing cell. After the selected exact factorization has been completed, apply the canonical reported-support selector only to whole normalized packets. The separated part of the two-root functional has an exact disjoint decomposition b Qsep r,M (g) = X k≥0 FM,k(g) (190) whose coefficients are C2 on the relative interior of that cell. Every normalization, denominator, localiza- tion, irregular and source-motion contact belongs to exactly one selected packet. No assertion that the individual coefficients FM,k agree for two different stopping factorizations is needed or made. Proof. At fixed finite volume and depth, the reporting-telescope and owner-map identities form a disjoint finite or absolutely convergent inventory of complete normalized packets. Their canonical physical reported supports therefore admit disjoint scalar dyadic selectors after packet completion. Inside one crossing cell the stopping depth and exact factorization are fixed, so the graph-segment C2 identity and the finite marked forest permit termwise differentiation. Summing the selectors gives the unselected exact functional, proving (190). Definition A.6 (Finite directed continuation and crossing cells). Fix the physical weak scale r, its endpoint gr = √ ur, and an arbitrary finite further directed depth M. Choose one terminal coarse side Mout and set Mi = 2M−i Mout. The side is first chosen admissible for Lemma 6.1 and is enlarged once, independently of M, to satisfy the fixed-torus smallness threshold of Theorem A.9. For every 0 ≤ g ≤ gr, use the exact weak recurrence u0(g) = g2 , ui+1(g) = Fi(ui(g)), gi(g) = p ui(g), 0 ≤ i < M. Let NM (g) := min{0 ≤ i ≤ M : ui(g) ≥ ū}, with NM (g) = ∞ if the set is empty. Uniform outgoing transversality implies that, when NM (g) < ∞, ui(g) < ū (i < NM (g)), uNM (g)(g) ∈ I⋆. The relative interiors of the sets on which NM (g) = N are called the crossing cells. On such a cell the exact factorization consists of N weak conditionals followed by the printed finite macrobridge and joint compact–optical logarithm. On the no-crossing cell it consists of all M weak conditionals followed by the terminal weak logarithm on the fixed coarse torus. 96 This is a choice of exact factorization, not a modification of the measure. The integer NM (g) is never differentiated. Derivatives are taken inside one crossing cell, where the depth is fixed, and are extended to cell boundaries using the original finite-volume C2 normalized functional. At g = 0 one always uses the no-crossing factorization and later sends M → ∞. Definition A.7 (Canonical finite-volume off-graph coordinates). For the directed schedule in Defini- tion A.6, put εi := e−σMi , δi := δG(Mi), Mi = 2M−i Mout. The first quantity records genuine wrapping histories in the interaction; the second records the scalar massless finite-size comparison. After each complete connected output has been formed and assigned its canonical reported hull, let Pwr i select precisely the history certificates containing at least one periodic wrapping occurrence at some scale not later than i. If Ri is the exact finite-volume centered interaction and R⋆(ui) is the rooted-thermodynamic invariant graph, set Ωi := Ri − R⋆(ui), Wi := Pwr i Ωi, Bi := (I − Pwr i )Ωi. (191) Thus Ωi = Wi + Bi coefficientwise. Both Ri and R⋆(ui) are written in the same exact schedule-fixed vacuum and tangent-normalized coupling coordinates. The coordinate intertwining and supportwise extraction identity therefore give E [2] X Ωi,X = 0 for every reported support X; all three off-graph coordinates lie in the actual centered stable fiber. The first coordinate is the canonical sum of histories carrying a wrapping certificate. After the tangent-normalized scalar coordinate is extracted, the massless acoustic sum–integral correction belongs to the nonautonomous scalar map Fi and is not an off-graph activity. By the exact regular interaction comparison and label recombination, the only nonwrapping off-graph difference is the slaved U4 charged/atlas complement; this is Bi and is exponentially flat in ui. No flat charged coefficient is identified with a wrapping history. A.2 Weak recurrence, wrapping control, and crossing cells Lemma A.8 (Full rooted finite-torus stable-map comparison). Let Φi(u, R) be the exact scheduled one-step map on the centered stable interaction after the vacuum, complete two-jet and tangent-normalized coupling coordinates have been extracted, and let Φ∞(u, R) be the rooted-thermodynamic map in the same coordinates. In the marked operator row, the coefficient of the same-order marked input is still C{εi + u−P i e−c0/ui }; every term in which a mark hits the map, the scalar trajectory, a projector or a newly created periodic atom is a lower-order forcing term bounded by Cm(i + 2)pm εiu 1−m/2 i + Cm(i + 2)pm u−Pm i e−c0/ui , m ≤ 2. (196) All constants are uniform in cutoff, original volume and finite directed depth. Proof. Use the complete labelled connected-history representation before forgetting owners. In the scheduled torus and in the rooted thermodynamic step, pair a history whenever its canonically reported hull has an injective periodic lift and contains no charged atlas label. The local Karcher, Gaussian, coarea, determinant, localization, extraction and collect templates are then literally the same, so paired histories and their derivatives cancel coefficientwise. An unpaired regular history has a nontrivial periodic cycle; its reported hull has length at least cMi. Weakening the permanent rooted support exponent once gives Ce−σ0Mi . An unpaired charged history belongs to the exactly recombined U4 row and is Cu−P e−c0/u . For (193), insert H as one distinguished input atom before the connected logarithm. The permanent one-step derivative envelope is a rooted absolute sum with that atom retained once. The same periodic-lift pairing applies, and the long hull still pays e−σ0Mi ; no derivative can shorten the already assigned reported hull. Vacuum, two-jet and coupling extraction are finite-range linear maps and are paired in the same way. 97 Passing to the adapted norm is harmless: that norm is a fixed finite sum of rooted norms of powers of the rooted-thermodynamic linear map, and each power preserves the preceding paired/unpaired classification. At R = R⋆(u), the free term cancels because both maps are written in the same schedule-fixed vacuum coordinates. The stable tangent uH1 is exponentially local but need not have finite support, so an unpaired periodic image may already be of order εiu. This sharp row gives (192); no quadratic stable cancellation is asserted. If the output is projected to the wrapping ideal, a charged history without a prior periodic certificate must also acquire a new nontrivial periodic cycle. It therefore pays both the charged flat row and εi+1. The same observation applied to a nonwrapping distinguished input proves (194) and (195). These are statements about the full centered interaction map, not inferences from the scalar comparison. Finally differentiate the labelled identities before summing histories. A g mark either stays on the distinguished input or hits one of finitely many local atoms, the scalar orbit, the extraction projector, or a new periodic connector. In the first case the same pairing proves the same-order coefficient in the statement. In every other case the marked child row and the trajectory ledger give the polynomial factor, while the periodic hull or charged event remains present. This proves (196) through two marks. A.3 Twice-marked conditional packets and even-contact contraction Lemma A.10 (Cellwise half-power and two-mark ledger). There are constants Cm and finite exponents pm such that, uniformly in M, in the crossing cell, and in every index before the selected terminal factor, i X h=0 uh(g) ≤ C log(i + 2), i X h=0 |gh(g)| ≤ C √ i + 1, (206) |∂m g ui(g)| + |∂m g gi(g)| ≤ Cm(i + 2)pm , m = 0, 1, 2. (207) The same estimates hold on the no-crossing cell through i = M. Proof. On a cell with crossing depth N, the reciprocal comparison from Theorem 6.4 gives ui(g) ≤ C(N − i + A)−1 before the crossing. On the no-crossing cell the direct fixed-schedule estimate (201) gives the same row with N replaced by M; no extension of the nonautonomous schedule is made. Summation gives (206). 99 For crossing cells use the printed marked C3 orbit row. For the no-crossing cell use (199). In either case every Fi fixes zero, has positive derivative, and can be written as Fi(u) = uϕi(u) with ϕi positive and uniformly C2 . Its finite composition therefore factors as ui(g) = g2 ψi(g2 ) with ψi > 0. Differentiating the exact recurrence twice and using F′ i (ui) ≤ 1 + Cui and |F′′ i (ui)| ≤ C gives polynomial growth in i. Consequently gi(g) = g p ψi(g2) is an odd C2 function and no 1/g singularity occurs at the Gaussian endpoint. The interaction marks required by the finite conditional forest are simultaneously controlled by (203) and (204). All constants are uniform on the compact first-crossing interval and on the enlarged no-crossing weak wedge. Lemma A.11 (Exact finite-depth twice-marked conditional forest). At finite directed depth M, differen- tiate the exact factorization of Definition A.6 with respect to the two curvature roots and at most two copies of g. The result is a finite sum of complete scale-labelled packet trees such that: (i) the two source leaves have one unique lowest common ancestor; (ii) each g mark is owned exactly once by a trajectory coordinate, a conditional logarithm, a source return, the terminal weak logarithm, or the compact handoff logarithm; (iii) every covariance edge, hard-core derivative, field factorial, regulator allocation and normalization contact belongs to one complete one-step packet before multiscale composition; (iv) the coefficients are exactly those of the Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula. No infinite-depth passage is asserted in this lemma. Proof. Apply the joint-cumulant derivative identity to every exact normalized weak conditional and, on a crossing cell, to the exact finite macrobridge and joint compact–optical logarithm. Then apply the Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula to the finite composition. This assertion is coefficientwise and uses only finite-volume differentiability; the uniform compact marked bound is supplied later by Theorem A.20. The one-step summed replica–BKAR theorem controls each weak packet, Lemma A.4 controls zero, one and two g marks, and finite source transport controls the external roots. Uniqueness of the lowest common ancestor is the ordinary tree property. Lemma A.12 (Top marked ancestor and complete-packet shell). In a packet tree from Lemma A.11, contract every maximal descendant subtree carrying no distinguished label into its exact parent-normalized decoration, and suppress every maximal unary chain into its exact ordered transfer product. The distin- guished labels are the two curvature roots and the zero, one or two active g marks. They have a unique lowest common ancestor v⊤; let j be its conditional scale. Call v⊤ the top marked ancestor. All vertices strictly above v⊤ are unmarked. The complete coefficient created at v⊤ includes every contracted side decoration with its canonical reported support. Decompose these whole coefficients by their full reported-support shell k using a scalar translation- and reflection-invariant selector. The selected class is therefore a complete BRS/reflection packet with every denominator and normalization contact still inside it. If j ≤ k + C0, the permanent rooted-support row of that whole packet gives C(1 + 2k−j )q e−c2k−j . (208) If j > k + C0, the full support lies inside a fixed enlargement of one scale-j cell and no spatial connector estimate is needed. Proof. Contraction of an unlabelled descendant is its exact conditional value, so it retains its canonical reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the parity transfer product. The unique lowest common ancestor of all labels is an ordinary tree invariant. If a g derivative lands on a coarser conditional map, its mark is attached at that map and therefore moves v⊤ to that scale. Hence no marked vertex can occur above v⊤. The shell selector is applied only after the full packet at v⊤ has been collected. A remote unmarked side decoration is not cut from its parent and is not asked to carry an external Taylor factor. If exact localization turns its contribution into a smaller-hull contact, that term is already in the finite contact block and is not a shell-k packet. Otherwise its canonical reported support remains part of the whole packet. When that support spans 2k−j scale-j blocks, the strict rooted-support norm and the weak connector theorem give (208), including covariance, hard-core and mixed alternatives. The exact Loc/collect identity of Theorem 5.8 ensures that this dichotomy is made before the stable packet is declared. 100 Theorem A.13 (Complete rooted even-contact contraction). Compatibility of the physical scale with the common diagonal. Let f be the profile of Lemma 10.1, and let the constants Cf , pf be those in the uniform endpoint and source-defect rows above. Choose the fixed countable source-scale set used in Corollary 8.12 to contain a cofinal dyadic sequence rn ↓ 0. There is an index n such that, for r = rn, Cf  u(r) + r2 L(r)pf  ≤ 1 2 V0(f). Before taking the common diagonal subsequence, enlarge the countable source algebra by the fixed fields Yr(f), ΘYr(f), and their reflected product. The same single diagonal extraction then gives all Schwinger limits entering their OS form and preserves every cutoff- and volume-uniform lower bound. The scale r is not changed after extraction. The cofinal dyadic choice is countable, while u(r) → 0 and r2 L(r)pf → 0 along it. Hence one r satisfies the displayed inequality. Adding finitely many fields at that scale preserves countability of the source algebra. The source-tree theorem and Corollary 8.12 therefore permit the same diagonal argument, and convergence of the finitely many Schwinger functions passes a uniform finite-cutoff lower bound to the limit. Theorem 10.5 (Interacting-to-Gaussian reflected two-root comparison). For the profile of Lemma 10.1, uniformly in cutoff, volume, and preceding ultraviolet depth, |Sur (ΘYr(f)Yr(f)) − V0(f)| ≤ Cf  u(r) + r2 L(r)pf  . (186) Consequently, for sufficiently small fixed dyadic r, ∥[Yr(f)]∥2 OS ≥ 1 2 V0(f) > 0. (187) Proof. The master endpoint theorem, equivalently Theorem 10.2, and evenness give | b Qr(gr) − b Qr(0)| ≤ Z gr 0 Cf g dg ≤ Cf ur. At g = 0 the law is the grounded tangent Gaussian and the principal source is the Gaussian Wick source, so b Qr(0) = V0(f). Add the dimension-six source-defect estimate below; its source error is Cf r2 L(r)pf . Choose r and the common subsequence by the compatibility argument above; the total error is then at most V0(f)/2. Reflection-positive recombination identifies the finite-cutoff form with the squared OS norm, and the same lemma passes the lower bound to the common limit. 10.4 Limiting curvature vector and finite spectral window Lemma 10.6 (Survival of the reflected curvature vector in the common limit). Let Yr(f) be a fixed positive-time reflected local curvature functional. Suppose along the common diagonal subsequence: (i) every Schwinger function entering Sa,L(ΘYr(f)Yr(f)) converges; (ii) reflection-positive recombination has already been performed; (iii) uniformly in cutoff and volume, Sa,L(ΘYr(f)Yr(f)) ≥ 1 2 V0(f) > 0. Then the limiting Osterwalder–Schrader vector ψY = [Yr(f)] is nonzero and satisfies ∥ψY ∥2 OS = lim Sa,L(ΘYr(f)Yr(f)) ≥ 1 2 V0(f) > 0. 93 Proof. The OS norm of this fixed local polynomial is a finite linear combination of the convergent limiting Schwinger functions. Therefore its finite-cutoff values converge to the limiting quadratic form. The uniform positive lower bound passes to the limit. Corollary 10.7 (Finite positive local spectral window without Wilson pushforward). Let ψY be the nonzero limiting curvature vector from Lemma 10.6. If common physical clustering and the OS spectral argument give supp µY ⊂ [m⋆, ∞), m⋆ > 0, then there exists a finite MY ≥ m⋆ such that µY ([m⋆, MY ]) > 0. No exact Wilson pushforward, Wilson moment identity, or reducing retained Hilbert subspace is used. Proof. The finite spectral measure has total mass 0 < µY ([m⋆, ∞)) = ∥ψY ∥2 OS < ∞. Since [m⋆, n] ↑ [m⋆, ∞), continuity from below implies that some finite n has positive mass. Set MY = n. 11 Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups Theorem 11.1 (Completion of the fixed-group construction). For every fixed compact connected simple G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds. Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors with Ig, so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus, with the universal color factor CA(G). The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG, and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam, reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction, and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG, the first proof call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is color-free, tensors with Ig, or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space. Corollary 11.2 (The SU(N) family). For every fixed integer N ≥ 2, the theorem holds for G = SU(N) in the defining representation, and the scale-two map is u(2L) = u(L) + 11N log 2 24π2 u(L)2 + ON (u(L)3 ). No estimate uniform as N → ∞ is asserted. Remark 11.3 (Logical scope). The construction is a fixed-group theorem. The directed subsequence, curvature-jet observable scope, and absence of a large-rank uniformity claim are part of the statement, not omitted conclusions. A Endpoint packet-shell verification This appendix supplies the detailed proof of Theorem 10.4. It is part of the same manuscript and uses the standing uniformity convention of Section 1. The main text isolates the logical endpoint theorem; the statements below retain the full finite-volume, crossing-cell, pre-owner, compact-entrance, and terminal-shell verification needed for that theorem. 94 A.1 Grounded covariance, contact subtraction, and weak admission Lemma A.1 (Grounded-torus curvature covariance). Let C (L) r be the zero-mode-removed transverse covariance remaining after the weak flow is stopped at scale r on a periodic torus of side L ≥ 4r. For j = 0, 1, 2, |∇j C(L) r (x, y)| ≤ Cjr−2−j (1 + dL(x, y)/r)−(2+j) , with constants independent of L. The corresponding AA, AF, and FF rows have decay orders two, three, and four. Proof. Write C (L) r = R ∞ r2 (e−t∆L − Π0)ΠT dt. For t ≤ L2 , periodization gives |K (L) t (x, y) − L−4 | ≤ Ct−2 e−cdL(x,y)2 /t + CL−4 , and derivative rows without the constant term. For t ≥ L2 , use the nonzero spectral gap. The transverse projector commutes with the heat semigroup and has the standard scalar-plus-two-derivative representation. Integration gives the stated rows; the scalar L−4 contribution is O(L−2 ) and is absorbed because dL ≤ 2L. Definition A.2 (Globally subtracted even packet kernels). Let Tab µν(p) be the complete two-open-leg coefficient of the normalized generating functional after all longitudinal, ghost, determinant, normalization, and source contacts have been included. The background Ward identity identifies its exact local contact T(0). Define Tren (p) = T(p) − T(0) before any absolute value is taken. A packet selector is a scalar, color-blind, translation-covariant and reflection-invariant function of the exact conditional scale labels and reported support geometry. Apply such a selector only after the whole BRS/reflection packet and its normalization contacts have been collected. If T = P λ Tλ is the resulting disjoint packet decomposition, absolute convergence gives the exact allocation Tren (p) = X λ Tλ(p) − Tλ(0)  . No cut through an individual packet and no shellwise Ward identity is used. Lemma A.3 (Exact even-contact division of complete packets). For every complete packet class λ, the one-open-leg coefficient vanishes by global color invariance. The renormalized two-open-leg packet is reflection even, vanishes at p = 0, and admits the exact second-order division Tλ(p) − Tλ(0) = X α,β b pα b pβSλ,αβ(p). (188) For every 0 < a′ < a0, the rooted norm of Sλ is bounded by the second support moment of the same complete packet at exponent a0. If the complete packet is represented at weak scale i, the two displayed difference factors carry the canonical relative factor (r/ℓi)2 = 2−2i (189) with respect to the fixed physical source scale r. This factor belongs to the whole packet. It is not assigned to a chosen branch or attachment cut. Proof. The selector is inserted only after the exact normalized packet is formed and is scalar under color, translation and aligned reflection. Hence every selected sum is again a complete packet. Its one-open-leg coefficient is an invariant adjoint vector and therefore vanishes. Global contact subtraction is allocated after the disjoint packet decomposition, so each selected renormalized coefficient vanishes at p = 0. Reflection gives Tλ(−p) = Tλ(p) and therefore removes the linear Taylor term. The integral Taylor formula, or equivalently finite Laurent telescoping in the variables eipα , gives (188). Two momentum derivatives insert two support displacements, absorbed by the unused exponent a0 − a′ . The divided packet has no reflection-even scalar or rank-two local component of engineering dimension four or five. Lemmas 5.22 and 5.23 therefore place its local projection in the stable degree-six block; this observation will be used only for stable membership, not to estimate the nonlocal packet. The scale factor in (189) is instead purely geometric. In the tangent-normalized profile coordinates, one lattice difference at representation scale ℓi = 2i r is (r/ℓi) times the corresponding unit-scale difference. The two explicit factors in (188) therefore contribute exactly (r/ℓi)2 = 2−2i . The two support moments of Sλ are absorbed by the unused rooted exponent. This argument applies to the whole packet, local and nonlocal together. Since the division identity holds for the entire C2 family, zero, one or two g derivatives do not remove the two explicit differences. 95 Lemma A.4 (g-marked one-scale admission). There is g0 > 0 such that, for real 0 ≤ g ≤ g0, the complete normalized interaction and all declared source/defect marks are twice continuously differentiable in the permanent regulator space. An undistinguished non-Gaussian child has parent-normalized row η0(g) ≤ Cg, while a child carrying one or two distinguished g derivatives has row η1(g) + η2(g) ≤ C. The exactly recombined large-field/irregular row and its first two g derivatives extend continuously by zero at g = 0 and are bounded by Cmg−pm e−c/g2 for m ≤ 2. No claim that an attached odd child is O(g2 ) is made. Proof. In the scale-normalized Gaussian coordinate, the cubic Yang–Mills species starts at order g, the quartic and other non-Gaussian even species start at order g2 , and the common species tube controls all field and source marks. Thus an unmarked species row is Cg, while one or two explicit g derivatives remove at most the corresponding powers and give a finite row. The regulator allocations are additive under multiplication, and the Gram and Pruefer estimates in the summed replica–BKAR theorem are insensitive to the parity of an attached tensor. The ordinary parent-normalized child recursion therefore closes for Cg0 below its printed threshold, with two fixed derivative marks handled by Leibniz. The C3 -flat irregular estimate in u = g2 gives the displayed first and second g-derivative rows after the chain rule. In particular the model child gA3 differentiated once by a covariance edge is correctly admitted as an O(g) child, and one g derivative of that child is O(1). Lemma A.5 (Cellwise complete shell decomposition). Fix the finite directed depth M and one crossing cell, including the no-crossing cell. After the selected exact factorization has been completed, apply the canonical reported-support selector only to whole normalized packets. The separated part of the two-root functional has an exact disjoint decomposition b Qsep r,M (g) = X k≥0 FM,k(g) (190) whose coefficients are C2 on the relative interior of that cell. Every normalization, denominator, localiza- tion, irregular and source-motion contact belongs to exactly one selected packet. No assertion that the individual coefficients FM,k agree for two different stopping factorizations is needed or made. Proof. At fixed finite volume and depth, the reporting-telescope and owner-map identities form a disjoint finite or absolutely convergent inventory of complete normalized packets. Their canonical physical reported supports therefore admit disjoint scalar dyadic selectors after packet completion. Inside one crossing cell the stopping depth and exact factorization are fixed, so the graph-segment C2 identity and the finite marked forest permit termwise differentiation. Summing the selectors gives the unselected exact functional, proving (190). Definition A.6 (Finite directed continuation and crossing cells). Fix the physical weak scale r, its endpoint gr = √ ur, and an arbitrary finite further directed depth M. Choose one terminal coarse side Mout and set Mi = 2M−i Mout. The side is first chosen admissible for Lemma 6.1 and is enlarged once, independently of M, to satisfy the fixed-torus smallness threshold of Theorem A.9. For every 0 ≤ g ≤ gr, use the exact weak recurrence u0(g) = g2 , ui+1(g) = Fi(ui(g)), gi(g) = p ui(g), 0 ≤ i < M. Let NM (g) := min{0 ≤ i ≤ M : ui(g) ≥ ū}, with NM (g) = ∞ if the set is empty. Uniform outgoing transversality implies that, when NM (g) < ∞, ui(g) < ū (i < NM (g)), uNM (g)(g) ∈ I⋆. The relative interiors of the sets on which NM (g) = N are called the crossing cells. On such a cell the exact factorization consists of N weak conditionals followed by the printed finite macrobridge and joint compact–optical logarithm. On the no-crossing cell it consists of all M weak conditionals followed by the terminal weak logarithm on the fixed coarse torus. 96 This is a choice of exact factorization, not a modification of the measure. The integer NM (g) is never differentiated. Derivatives are taken inside one crossing cell, where the depth is fixed, and are extended to cell boundaries using the original finite-volume C2 normalized functional. At g = 0 one always uses the no-crossing factorization and later sends M → ∞. Definition A.7 (Canonical finite-volume off-graph coordinates). For the directed schedule in Defini- tion A.6, put εi := e−σMi , δi := δG(Mi), Mi = 2M−i Mout. The first quantity records genuine wrapping histories in the interaction; the second records the scalar massless finite-size comparison. After each complete connected output has been formed and assigned its canonical reported hull, let Pwr i select precisely the history certificates containing at least one periodic wrapping occurrence at some scale not later than i. If Ri is the exact finite-volume centered interaction and R⋆(ui) is the rooted-thermodynamic invariant graph, set Ωi := Ri − R⋆(ui), Wi := Pwr i Ωi, Bi := (I − Pwr i )Ωi. (191) Thus Ωi = Wi + Bi coefficientwise. Both Ri and R⋆(ui) are written in the same exact schedule-fixed vacuum and tangent-normalized coupling coordinates. The coordinate intertwining and supportwise extraction identity therefore give E [2] X Ωi,X = 0 for every reported support X; all three off-graph coordinates lie in the actual centered stable fiber. The first coordinate is the canonical sum of histories carrying a wrapping certificate. After the tangent-normalized scalar coordinate is extracted, the massless acoustic sum–integral correction belongs to the nonautonomous scalar map Fi and is not an off-graph activity. By the exact regular interaction comparison and label recombination, the only nonwrapping off-graph difference is the slaved U4 charged/atlas complement; this is Bi and is exponentially flat in ui. No flat charged coefficient is identified with a wrapping history. A.2 Weak recurrence, wrapping control, and crossing cells Lemma A.8 (Full rooted finite-torus stable-map comparison). (196) All constants are uniform in cutoff, original volume and finite directed depth. Proof. Use the complete labelled connected-history representation before forgetting owners. In the scheduled torus and in the rooted thermodynamic step, pair a history whenever its canonically reported hull has an injective periodic lift and contains no charged atlas label. The local Karcher, Gaussian, coarea, determinant, localization, extraction and collect templates are then literally the same, so paired histories and their derivatives cancel coefficientwise. An unpaired regular history has a nontrivial periodic cycle; its reported hull has length at least cMi. Weakening the permanent rooted support exponent once gives Ce−σ0Mi . An unpaired charged history belongs to the exactly recombined U4 row and is Cu−P e−c0/u . For (193), insert H as one distinguished input atom before the connected logarithm. The permanent one-step derivative envelope is a rooted absolute sum with that atom retained once. Vacuum, two-jet and coupling extraction are finite-range linear maps and are paired in the same way. 97 Passing to the adapted norm is harmless: that norm is a fixed finite sum of rooted norms of powers of the rooted-thermodynamic linear map, and each power preserves the preceding paired/unpaired classification. At R = R⋆(u), the free term cancels because both maps are written in the same schedule-fixed vacuum coordinates. The stable tangent uH1 is exponentially local but need not have finite support, so an unpaired periodic image may already be of order εiu. This sharp row gives (192); no quadratic stable cancellation is asserted. If the output is projected to the wrapping ideal, a charged history without a prior periodic certificate must also acquire a new nontrivial periodic cycle. It therefore pays both the charged flat row and εi+1. The same observation applied to a nonwrapping distinguished input proves (194) and (195). These are statements about the full centered interaction map, not inferences from the scalar comparison. Finally differentiate the labelled identities before summing histories. A g mark either stays on the distinguished input or hits one of finitely many local atoms, the scalar orbit, the extraction projector, or a new periodic connector. In the first case the same pairing proves the same-order coefficient in the statement. In every other case the marked child row and the trajectory ledger give the polynomial factor, while the periodic hull or charged event remains present. This proves (196) through two marks. A.3 Twice-marked conditional packets and even-contact contraction Lemma A.10 (Cellwise half-power and two-mark ledger). There are constants Cm and finite exponents pm such that, uniformly in M, in the crossing cell, and in every index before the selected terminal factor, i X h=0 uh(g) ≤ C log(i + 2), i X h=0 |gh(g)| ≤ C √ i + 1, (206) |∂m g ui(g)| + |∂m g gi(g)| ≤ Cm(i + 2)pm , m = 0, 1, 2. (207) The same estimates hold on the no-crossing cell through i = M. Proof. On a cell with crossing depth N, the reciprocal comparison from Theorem 6.4 gives ui(g) ≤ C(N − i + A)−1 before the crossing. On the no-crossing cell the direct fixed-schedule estimate (201) gives the same row with N replaced by M; no extension of the nonautonomous schedule is made. Summation gives (206). 99 For crossing cells use the printed marked C3 orbit row. For the no-crossing cell use (199). In either case every Fi fixes zero, has positive derivative, and can be written as Fi(u) = uϕi(u) with ϕi positive and uniformly C2 . Its finite composition therefore factors as ui(g) = g2 ψi(g2 ) with ψi > 0. Differentiating the exact recurrence twice and using F′ i (ui) ≤ 1 + Cui and |F′′ i (ui)| ≤ C gives polynomial growth in i. Consequently gi(g) = g p ψi(g2) is an odd C2 function and no 1/g singularity occurs at the Gaussian endpoint. The interaction marks required by the finite conditional forest are simultaneously controlled by (203) and (204). All constants are uniform on the compact first-crossing interval and on the enlarged no-crossing weak wedge. Lemma A.11 (Exact finite-depth twice-marked conditional forest). At finite directed depth M, differen- tiate the exact factorization of Definition A.6 with respect to the two curvature roots and at most two copies of g. The result is a finite sum of complete scale-labelled packet trees such that: (i) the two source leaves have one unique lowest common ancestor; (ii) each g mark is owned exactly once by a trajectory coordinate, a conditional logarithm, a source return, the terminal weak logarithm, or the compact handoff logarithm; (iii) every covariance edge, hard-core derivative, field factorial, regulator allocation and normalization contact belongs to one complete one-step packet before multiscale composition; (iv) the coefficients are exactly those of the Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula. No infinite-depth passage is asserted in this lemma. Proof. Apply the joint-cumulant derivative identity to every exact normalized weak conditional and, on a crossing cell, to the exact finite macrobridge and joint compact–optical logarithm. Then apply the Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula to the finite composition. This assertion is coefficientwise and uses only finite-volume differentiability; the uniform compact marked bound is supplied later by Theorem A.20. The one-step summed replica–BKAR theorem controls each weak packet, Lemma A.4 controls zero, one and two g marks, and finite source transport controls the external roots. Uniqueness of the lowest common ancestor is the ordinary tree property. Lemma A.12 (Top marked ancestor and complete-packet shell). In a packet tree from Lemma A.11, contract every maximal descendant subtree carrying no distinguished label into its exact parent-normalized decoration, and suppress every maximal unary chain into its exact ordered transfer product. The distin- guished labels are the two curvature roots and the zero, one or two active g marks. They have a unique lowest common ancestor v⊤; let j be its conditional scale. Call v⊤ the top marked ancestor. All vertices strictly above v⊤ are unmarked. The complete coefficient created at v⊤ includes every contracted side decoration with its canonical reported support. Decompose these whole coefficients by their full reported-support shell k using a scalar translation- and reflection-invariant selector. The selected class is therefore a complete BRS/reflection packet with every denominator and normalization contact still inside it. If j ≤ k + C0, the permanent rooted-support row of that whole packet gives C(1 + 2k−j )q e−c2k−j . (208) If j > k + C0, the full support lies inside a fixed enlargement of one scale-j cell and no spatial connector estimate is needed. Proof. Contraction of an unlabelled descendant is its exact conditional value, so it retains its canonical reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the parity transfer product. The unique lowest common ancestor of all labels is an ordinary tree invariant. If a g derivative lands on a coarser conditional map, its mark is attached at that map and therefore moves v⊤ to that scale. Hence no marked vertex can occur above v⊤. The shell selector is applied only after the full packet at v⊤ has been collected. A remote unmarked side decoration is not cut from its parent and is not asked to carry an external Taylor factor. If exact localization turns its contribution into a smaller-hull contact, that term is already in the finite contact block and is not a shell-k packet. Otherwise its canonical reported support remains part of the whole packet. When that support spans 2k−j scale-j blocks, the strict rooted-support norm and the weak connector theorem give (208), including covariance, hard-core and mixed alternatives. The exact Loc/collect identity of Theorem 5.8 ensures that this dichotomy is made before the stable packet is declared. 100 Theorem A.13 (Complete rooted even-contact contraction). Fix the permanent rooted support exponent and one passive-source order large enough for the two curvature roots. Let Yr(f) be a fixed positive-time reflected local curvature functional. Suppose along the common diagonal subsequence: (i) every Schwinger function entering Sa,L(ΘYr(f)Yr(f)) converges; (ii) reflection-positive recombination has already been performed; (iii) uniformly in cutoff and volume, Sa,L(ΘYr(f)Yr(f)) ≥ 1 2 V0(f) > 0.
