(210) The same estimate holds for an already formed zero-, one- or two-g-marked packet at the top marked ancestor, because every transfer above that ancestor is unmarked. Moreover the exact division gives, in the fixed physical source normalization, ∥K∥⋆,r ≤ C2−2j X α,β ∥Sαβ∥ (2) ⋆,r. (211) Thus the strict contraction concerns the entire complete rooted packet; it is not inferred from the finite local jet block alone. Proof. Apply exact collect and then the fixed covariant localization projector from Section 4.5. Every complete packet has the exact splitting K = ΠDK + (1 − ΠD)K. For K ∈ I (2) j , centering and the complete one-open-leg identity remove the lower scalar and adjoint source coordinates, global contact subtraction removes the reflection-even dimension-four source coordinate, reflection removes the linear profile jet, and Lemmas 5.22 and 5.23 remove the dimension-five local rows. Hence ΠDK belongs to the finite stable local jet block, beginning in degree six. The remainder (1 − ΠD)K is precisely the reported nonlocal/stable rooted remainder. Regular/irregular recombination, normalization contacts and finite source coordinates are already inside these two exact pieces. Theorem 4.30 and the adapted one-step mean-value contraction following (29) contract the sum of those two pieces, not merely its local projection, in one permanent rooted norm. Lemma 5.20, followed by the uniform mean-value estimate on the centered weak graph in Theorem 5.1, supplies one equivalent one-step norm and one ρ < 1 on the whole common weak tube. Those results were proved through three active structural marks and arbitrary fixed passive source order, so they apply to the present complete two-root packets and to already formed coupling derivatives. This proves (210) without promoting a finite-dimensional jet estimate to a nonlocal packet estimate. The exact normalized source transfer commutes with translations, global color and aligned reflection. A constant coarse profile pulls back to a constant fine profile. Therefore K(0) = 0 and reflection evenness are preserved, which proves (209). Finally, each displayed lattice profile difference at scale ℓj = 2j r is (r/ℓj) = 2−j times the unit physical difference. Two differences give 2−2j , while the two support moments are absorbed by the unused permanent exponent. This proves (211) directly on the whole packet. Lemma A.14 (Top-scale geometric shell row). Unique-coarsest-scale allocation Differentiate the exact composition of normalized conditional logarithms with respect to source parameters. Every connected two-buffer source history is assigned exactly once either to the matching connector or to one weak scale j ≥ jw. Finer decorations attached to its coarsest skeleton have a uniformly summable one/two-root norm. Put dw := CR + m−1 w . 75 For d ≥ dw, X j≥jw Ce−c0| log σ|(d−CR)/ℓj ≤ C′ e−mw(d−CR)/2 . (116) For d < dw the complete history is retained in one fixed enlarged local cluster: it is uniformly bounded for bounded observables and controlled in the local source topology for renormalized fields. Proof. By Corollary 8.7, the lowest common ancestor of the two source buffers assigns every connected tree to exactly one terminal or weak-scale class. This is the first scale in the exact recursive conditional- logarithm formula at which the two source components are joined. The least-label convention removes automorphism multiplicities before estimates are applied. The fixed root algebra sums every finer decoration independently of the number of active nonroot supports. Since ℓj = 2−(j−jw) ℓjw , put x = mw(d − CR)+. The scale sum is bounded by C P r≥0 exp(−2r x). The condition d ≥ dw gives x ≥ 1, and then this is at most C1 exp(−x), proving (116). If d < dw the two buffers lie in one fixed enlarged physical neighborhood. Their ultraviolet contact subtrees are not summed as separate long connectors; they are contracted into the exact local atom described in the statement. Theorem 8.9 (Uniform physical two-source connector). There are dloc, A0, m > 0, independent of resolution, microscopic cutoff, volume, and boundary conditions, with the following properties. (a) For two centered bounded positive-scale observables supported in BR(z) and BR(z′ ), the complete connected majorant is bounded at every separation by Cm(BR(z), BR(z′ )) ≤ Km(z, z′ ) := A0e−m(|z−z′ |1 −2R)+ , (117) and sup z ℓ4 m X z′ Km(z, z′ ) + sup z′ ℓ4 m X z Km(z, z′ ) ≤ K0. (118) (b) If either buffer carries a fixed finite renormalized local source, displacement mark, or rotation mark, then for test sources with disjoint supports at distance d ≥ dloc the connected functional is bounded by Cs,A,Be−md . At shorter distance it is controlled in the local Schwartz/source seminorm supplied by the triangular finite-jet renormalization; no cutoff-uniform pointwise estimate on the diagonal is claimed. The exponent m is common to both classes and to every fixed finite source family. Proof. Combine Lemma 8.5 with the exact forest allocation and the weak scale sum. For d ≥ dloc choose dloc so the variable x = mw(d − CR)+ in Lemma 8.8 is at least one. Weak histories then decay with rate at least mw/2, while matching histories decay with rate at least 1 2 min{mtr, msp}. Hence one may take the common exponent m = m∗ from (111); Lemma 8.2 shows explicitly that this exponent is not degraded by increasing the ultraviolet depth. For bounded observables, the covariance and every short-distance connected local atom are bounded by their sup norms, so enlarging A0 extends the bound to all separations. Lemma 8.1 and integrability of the physical exponential kernel give (118). For a normalized local source, the short-distance cluster is not estimated pointwise. It is exactly the local source coefficient renormalized by the finite triangular counterterm matrix; the finite triangular source theorem printed in Part V bounds it in a finite Schwartz seminorm. Once two source supports are separated, the lowest-common-ancestor edge lies outside both local clusters and the same connector exponent applies. Thus the theorem derives a cutoff-independent physical rate without suppressing or hiding contact terms. All-order source trees Let κm,n(z1, . . . , zn) = Cum(Om,z1 , . . . , Om,zn ). Lemma 8.10 (Labelled-tree extraction). Every connected source-labelled history admits a deterministic allocation to a spanning tree on {1, . . . , n}. An edge joining bounded positive-scale roots is bounded by Theorem 8.9(a). For renormalized local sources, every contact subtree is first contracted into one local renormalized vertex; an edge joining two disjoint vertices is then bounded by Theorem 8.9(b). Only the two endpoint clusters of one edge are retained as roots, and all previously exposed variables are exterior data. 76 Proof. Start with the least label and expose the connected history until it first reaches the least unseen label. Draw that edge, absorb the exposed component into the parent cluster, and repeat. Every new label is attached once. When an edge is estimated, Theorem 6.44 treats the two endpoint clusters as the only distinguished roots and is uniform in all already exposed exterior variables. Theorem 8.11 (All-order physical source-tree bound). For every fixed finite family of centered bounded positive-scale source types there is Cs < ∞ such that |κm,n(z1, . . . , zn)| ≤ n!Cn s X T ∈Tn Y {i,j}∈E(T ) Km(zi, zj), (119) uniformly in resolution, cutoff, volume, and boundary conditions. For every fixed finite family of renormalized local or marked sources, the corresponding smeared cumulants satisfy |Cum(Φm(f1), . . . , Φm(fn))| ≤ Cn n Y i=1 pN (fi) (120) for one finite Schwartz seminorm pN depending on the source types. If the source labels split into two groups with mutually separated supports, the connected cumulant has the additional common factor e−md . Proof. Differentiate the connected source logarithm and first collect each maximal contact subtree into one local root. Apply Lemma 8.10; Theorem 6.44 shows that no matching-stage derivative of order n is required, because each tree edge sees only its two endpoint clusters as distinguished roots. For bounded roots, every edge has the pointwise connector (117); excess attachments are summed by the unused strict KP margin, and the deterministic allocation costs at most the displayed factorial. For renormalized local sources, first contract every contact subtree into its finite local source vertex. The remaining edges join disjoint clusters and carry the separated-support exponential from Theorem 8.9(b). The local vertices are bounded by the triangular source-renormalization seminorms. Repeated Schur summation of the remaining edges, with one Schwartz L1 norm at the tree root, gives (120) and the separated-group exponential. Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence. If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are bounded by Ce−md with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3, together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11 permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed. In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation; factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition formula. Lemma 8.13 (OS linear-growth condition from the common source ball). For every fixed finite source multiplet there are a finite Schwartz seminorm pN , a radius ρN > 0, and CN < ∞ such that its limiting Schwinger distributions satisfy |Sn(f1, . . . , fn)| ≤ n! Cn N n Y i=1 pN (fi). The constants are compatible under the nested finite source multiplets. In particular the projective Schwinger family satisfies the OS linear-growth condition consumed in SI–5. Proof. Corollary 8.12 supplies one cutoff- and volume-independent analytic ball for the normalized source functional in the Banach norm induced by pN . Apply the one-variable Cauchy estimate to z 7→ W(z P i Ji) and polarize the resulting derivative bound. The common radius gives n!ρ−n N , and the finite incidence constants are absorbed in Cn N . The diagonal limit preserves the inequality, and the nested source construction preserves the constants after enlargement. 77 Lemma 8.14 (Reflection positivity inheritance under exact recombination). Every limiting positive-time quadratic form used in the common Schwinger system is the limit of a reflection-positive quadratic form under the original Wilson measure. Exact normal-chart, coarea, compact-pivot, and source transfor- mations are changes of variables or observable identities, not replacement probability measures, and all regular/irregular and half-cylinder labels are recombined before the OS form is taken. Therefore reflection positivity passes to the common limiting family. Proof. At finite cutoff and aligned reflection volume, let Fi be any finite family of constructed positive-time observables. Pull each Fi back through the exact block, fiber, and source identities to a gauge-invariant function of the original positive-time links. Exact recombination makes this pullback independent of the auxiliary labels. Reflection positivity of the Wilson measure gives X i,j cicj⟨ΘFi Fj⟩a,L ≥ 0. The equality is unchanged by the exact coordinate transformations because all Jacobian and normalization factors remain inside the same normalized integral once. Every entry of the finite matrix converges along the common subsequence, so the closed positive-semidefinite cone contains the limiting matrix. This proves the claim. Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered, permutation-symmetric, reflection-positive, continuously translation-covariant Schwinger distributions for the countable algebra generated by the bounded positive-scale observables and every fixed finite normalized local source family. The distributions are projectively compatible and satisfy uniform physical exponential clustering. Proof. The Schwartz estimate gives equicontinuity on each finite tensor power. Diagonal extraction over a countable dense test/source family gives the common projective system. Permutation symmetry passes as an algebraic identity, while Lemma 8.14 gives the complete reflection-positivity mechanism after exact label recombination. Riemann- sum translation errors vanish by the physical Schur bound, Corollary 8.12 gives clustering, and Lemma 8.13 gives the OS linear-growth row. 8.2 Ward identities and Euclidean covariance Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie algebra g: (I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space; (I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four; (I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection- even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density; (I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block. The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension. Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite- dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric tensor exists only for type Ar, and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions occur. 8.2.1 Global displacement source An explicit gauge-covariant link source Let Uρ(x) denote the link in a signed coordinate direction ρ. For every fixed compact connected simple G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds. Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and faithful-action constants of Section 2. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG, the first proof call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is color-free, tensors with Ig, or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space. Let C (L) r be the zero-mode-removed transverse covariance remaining after the weak flow is stopped at scale r on a periodic torus of side L ≥ 4r. For j = 0, 1, 2, |∇j C(L) r (x, y)| ≤ Cjr−2−j (1 + dL(x, y)/r)−(2+j) , with constants independent of L. The corresponding AA, AF, and FF rows have decay orders two, three, and four. Proof. Write C (L) r = R ∞ r2 (e−t∆L − Π0)ΠT dt. For t ≤ L2 , periodization gives |K (L) t (x, y) − L−4 | ≤ Ct−2 e−cdL(x,y)2 /t + CL−4 , and derivative rows without the constant term. For t ≥ L2 , use the nonzero spectral gap. The transverse projector commutes with the heat semigroup and has the standard scalar-plus-two-derivative representation. Integration gives the stated rows; the scalar L−4 contribution is O(L−2 ) and is absorbed because dL ≤ 2L. Definition A.2 (Globally subtracted even packet kernels). Let Tab µν(p) be the complete two-open-leg coefficient of the normalized generating functional after all longitudinal, ghost, determinant, normalization, and source contacts have been included. The background Ward identity identifies its exact local contact T(0). Define Tren (p) = T(p) − T(0) before any absolute value is taken. A packet selector is a scalar, color-blind, translation-covariant and reflection-invariant function of the exact conditional scale labels and reported support geometry. Apply such a selector only after the whole BRS/reflection packet and its normalization contacts have been collected. If T = P λ Tλ is the resulting disjoint packet decomposition, absolute convergence gives the exact allocation Tren (p) = X λ Tλ(p) − Tλ(0)  . No cut through an individual packet and no shellwise Ward identity is used. Lemma A.3 (Exact even-contact division of complete packets). For every complete packet class λ, the one-open-leg coefficient vanishes by global color invariance. The renormalized two-open-leg packet is reflection even, vanishes at p = 0, and admits the exact second-order division Tλ(p) − Tλ(0) = X α,β b pα b pβSλ,αβ(p). (188) For every 0 < a′ < a0, the rooted norm of Sλ is bounded by the second support moment of the same complete packet at exponent a0. If the complete packet is represented at weak scale i, the two displayed difference factors carry the canonical relative factor (r/ℓi)2 = 2−2i (189) with respect to the fixed physical source scale r. This factor belongs to the whole packet. It is not assigned to a chosen branch or attachment cut. Proof. The selector is inserted only after the exact normalized packet is formed and is scalar under color, translation and aligned reflection. Hence every selected sum is again a complete packet. Its one-open-leg coefficient is an invariant adjoint vector and therefore vanishes. Global contact subtraction is allocated after the disjoint packet decomposition, so each selected renormalized coefficient vanishes at p = 0. Reflection gives Tλ(−p) = Tλ(p) and therefore removes the linear Taylor term. The integral Taylor formula, or equivalently finite Laurent telescoping in the variables eipα , gives (188). Two momentum derivatives insert two support displacements, absorbed by the unused exponent a0 − a′ . The divided packet has no reflection-even scalar or rank-two local component of engineering dimension four or five. Lemmas 5.22 and 5.23 therefore place its local projection in the stable degree-six block; this observation will be used only for stable membership, not to estimate the nonlocal packet. The scale factor in (189) is instead purely geometric. In the tangent-normalized profile coordinates, one lattice difference at representation scale ℓi = 2i r is (r/ℓi) times the corresponding unit-scale difference. The two explicit factors in (188) therefore contribute exactly (r/ℓi)2 = 2−2i . The two support moments of Sλ are absorbed by the unused rooted exponent. This argument applies to the whole packet, local and nonlocal together. Since the division identity holds for the entire C2 family, zero, one or two g derivatives do not remove the two explicit differences. 95 Lemma A.4 (g-marked one-scale admission). There is g0 > 0 such that, for real 0 ≤ g ≤ g0, the complete normalized interaction and all declared source/defect marks are twice continuously differentiable in the permanent regulator space. An undistinguished non-Gaussian child has parent-normalized row η0(g) ≤ Cg, while a child carrying one or two distinguished g derivatives has row η1(g) + η2(g) ≤ C. The exactly recombined large-field/irregular row and its first two g derivatives extend continuously by zero at g = 0 and are bounded by Cmg−pm e−c/g2 for m ≤ 2. No claim that an attached odd child is O(g2 ) is made. Proof. In the scale-normalized Gaussian coordinate, the cubic Yang–Mills species starts at order g, the quartic and other non-Gaussian even species start at order g2 , and the common species tube controls all field and source marks. Thus an unmarked species row is Cg, while one or two explicit g derivatives remove at most the corresponding powers and give a finite row. The regulator allocations are additive under multiplication, and the Gram and Pruefer estimates in the summed replica–BKAR theorem are insensitive to the parity of an attached tensor. The ordinary parent-normalized child recursion therefore closes for Cg0 below its printed threshold, with two fixed derivative marks handled by Leibniz. The C3 -flat irregular estimate in u = g2 gives the displayed first and second g-derivative rows after the chain rule. In particular the model child gA3 differentiated once by a covariance edge is correctly admitted as an O(g) child, and one g derivative of that child is O(1). Lemma A.5 (Cellwise complete shell decomposition). Fix the finite directed depth M and one crossing cell, including the no-crossing cell. After the selected exact factorization has been completed, apply the canonical reported-support selector only to whole normalized packets. The separated part of the two-root functional has an exact disjoint decomposition b Qsep r,M (g) = X k≥0 FM,k(g) (190) whose coefficients are C2 on the relative interior of that cell. Every normalization, denominator, localiza- tion, irregular and source-motion contact belongs to exactly one selected packet. No assertion that the individual coefficients FM,k agree for two different stopping factorizations is needed or made. Proof. At fixed finite volume and depth, the reporting-telescope and owner-map identities form a disjoint finite or absolutely convergent inventory of complete normalized packets. Their canonical physical reported supports therefore admit disjoint scalar dyadic selectors after packet completion. Inside one crossing cell the stopping depth and exact factorization are fixed, so the graph-segment C2 identity and the finite marked forest permit termwise differentiation. Summing the selectors gives the unselected exact functional, proving (190). Definition A.6 (Finite directed continuation and crossing cells). Fix the physical weak scale r, its endpoint gr = √ ur, and an arbitrary finite further directed depth M. Choose one terminal coarse side Mout and set Mi = 2M−i Mout. The side is first chosen admissible for Lemma 6.1 and is enlarged once, independently of M, to satisfy the fixed-torus smallness threshold of Theorem A.9. For every 0 ≤ g ≤ gr, use the exact weak recurrence u0(g) = g2 , ui+1(g) = Fi(ui(g)), gi(g) = p ui(g), 0 ≤ i < M. Let NM (g) := min{0 ≤ i ≤ M : ui(g) ≥ ū}, with NM (g) = ∞ if the set is empty. Uniform outgoing transversality implies that, when NM (g) < ∞, ui(g) < ū (i < NM (g)), uNM (g)(g) ∈ I⋆. The relative interiors of the sets on which NM (g) = N are called the crossing cells. On such a cell the exact factorization consists of N weak conditionals followed by the printed finite macrobridge and joint compact–optical logarithm. On the no-crossing cell it consists of all M weak conditionals followed by the terminal weak logarithm on the fixed coarse torus. 96 This is a choice of exact factorization, not a modification of the measure. The integer NM (g) is never differentiated. Derivatives are taken inside one crossing cell, where the depth is fixed, and are extended to cell boundaries using the original finite-volume C2 normalized functional. At g = 0 one always uses the no-crossing factorization and later sends M → ∞. Definition A.7 (Canonical finite-volume off-graph coordinates). For the directed schedule in Defini- tion A.6, put εi := e−σMi , δi := δG(Mi), Mi = 2M−i Mout. The first quantity records genuine wrapping histories in the interaction; the second records the scalar massless finite-size comparison. After each complete connected output has been formed and assigned its canonical reported hull, let Pwr i select precisely the history certificates containing at least one periodic wrapping occurrence at some scale not later than i. If Ri is the exact finite-volume centered interaction and R⋆(ui) is the rooted-thermodynamic invariant graph, set Ωi := Ri − R⋆(ui), Wi := Pwr i Ωi, Bi := (I − Pwr i )Ωi. (191) Thus Ωi = Wi + Bi coefficientwise. Both Ri and R⋆(ui) are written in the same exact schedule-fixed vacuum and tangent-normalized coupling coordinates. The coordinate intertwining and supportwise extraction identity therefore give E [2] X Ωi,X = 0 for every reported support X; all three off-graph coordinates lie in the actual centered stable fiber. The first coordinate is the canonical sum of histories carrying a wrapping certificate. After the tangent-normalized scalar coordinate is extracted, the massless acoustic sum–integral correction belongs to the nonautonomous scalar map Fi and is not an off-graph activity. By the exact regular interaction comparison and label recombination, the only nonwrapping off-graph difference is the slaved U4 charged/atlas complement; this is Bi and is exponentially flat in ui. No flat charged coefficient is identified with a wrapping history. A.2 Weak recurrence, wrapping control, and crossing cells Lemma A.8 (Full rooted finite-torus stable-map comparison). Let Φi(u, R) be the exact scheduled one-step map on the centered stable interaction after the vacuum, complete two-jet and tangent-normalized coupling coordinates have been extracted, and let Φ∞(u, R) be the rooted-thermodynamic map in the same coordinates. In the marked operator row, the coefficient of the same-order marked input is still C{εi + u−P i e−c0/ui }; every term in which a mark hits the map, the scalar trajectory, a projector or a newly created periodic atom is a lower-order forcing term bounded by Cm(i + 2)pm εiu 1−m/2 i + Cm(i + 2)pm u−Pm i e−c0/ui , m ≤ 2. (196) All constants are uniform in cutoff, original volume and finite directed depth. Proof. Use the complete labelled connected-history representation before forgetting owners. In the scheduled torus and in the rooted thermodynamic step, pair a history whenever its canonically reported hull has an injective periodic lift and contains no charged atlas label. The local Karcher, Gaussian, coarea, determinant, localization, extraction and collect templates are then literally the same, so paired histories and their derivatives cancel coefficientwise. An unpaired regular history has a nontrivial periodic cycle; its reported hull has length at least cMi. Weakening the permanent rooted support exponent once gives Ce−σ0Mi . An unpaired charged history belongs to the exactly recombined U4 row and is Cu−P e−c0/u . For (193), insert H as one distinguished input atom before the connected logarithm. The permanent one-step derivative envelope is a rooted absolute sum with that atom retained once. The same periodic-lift pairing applies, and the long hull still pays e−σ0Mi ; no derivative can shorten the already assigned reported hull. Vacuum, two-jet and coupling extraction are finite-range linear maps and are paired in the same way. 97 Passing to the adapted norm is harmless: that norm is a fixed finite sum of rooted norms of powers of the rooted-thermodynamic linear map, and each power preserves the preceding paired/unpaired classification. At R = R⋆(u), the free term cancels because both maps are written in the same schedule-fixed vacuum coordinates. The stable tangent uH1 is exponentially local but need not have finite support, so an unpaired periodic image may already be of order εiu. This sharp row gives (192); no quadratic stable cancellation is asserted. If the output is projected to the wrapping ideal, a charged history without a prior periodic certificate must also acquire a new nontrivial periodic cycle. It therefore pays both the charged flat row and εi+1. The same observation applied to a nonwrapping distinguished input proves (194) and (195). These are statements about the full centered interaction map, not inferences from the scalar comparison. Finally differentiate the labelled identities before summing histories. A g mark either stays on the distinguished input or hits one of finitely many local atoms, the scalar orbit, the extraction projector, or a new periodic connector. In the first case the same pairing proves the same-order coefficient in the statement. In every other case the marked child row and the trajectory ledger give the polynomial factor, while the periodic hull or charged event remains present. This proves (196) through two marks. A.3 Twice-marked conditional packets and even-contact contraction Lemma A.10 (Cellwise half-power and two-mark ledger). There are constants Cm and finite exponents pm such that, uniformly in M, in the crossing cell, and in every index before the selected terminal factor, i X h=0 uh(g) ≤ C log(i + 2), i X h=0 |gh(g)| ≤ C √ i + 1, (206) |∂m g ui(g)| + |∂m g gi(g)| ≤ Cm(i + 2)pm , m = 0, 1, 2. (207) The same estimates hold on the no-crossing cell through i = M. Proof. On a cell with crossing depth N, the reciprocal comparison from Theorem 6.4 gives ui(g) ≤ C(N − i + A)−1 before the crossing. On the no-crossing cell the direct fixed-schedule estimate (201) gives the same row with N replaced by M; no extension of the nonautonomous schedule is made. Summation gives (206). 99 For crossing cells use the printed marked C3 orbit row. For the no-crossing cell use (199). In either case every Fi fixes zero, has positive derivative, and can be written as Fi(u) = uϕi(u) with ϕi positive and uniformly C2 . Its finite composition therefore factors as ui(g) = g2 ψi(g2 ) with ψi > 0. Differentiating the exact recurrence twice and using F′ i (ui) ≤ 1 + Cui and |F′′ i (ui)| ≤ C gives polynomial growth in i. Consequently gi(g) = g p ψi(g2) is an odd C2 function and no 1/g singularity occurs at the Gaussian endpoint. The interaction marks required by the finite conditional forest are simultaneously controlled by (203) and (204). All constants are uniform on the compact first-crossing interval and on the enlarged no-crossing weak wedge. Lemma A.11 (Exact finite-depth twice-marked conditional forest). At finite directed depth M, differen- tiate the exact factorization of Definition A.6 with respect to the two curvature roots and at most two copies of g. The result is a finite sum of complete scale-labelled packet trees such that: (i) the two source leaves have one unique lowest common ancestor; (ii) each g mark is owned exactly once by a trajectory coordinate, a conditional logarithm, a source return, the terminal weak logarithm, or the compact handoff logarithm; (iii) every covariance edge, hard-core derivative, field factorial, regulator allocation and normalization contact belongs to one complete one-step packet before multiscale composition; (iv) the coefficients are exactly those of the Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula. No infinite-depth passage is asserted in this lemma. Proof. Apply the joint-cumulant derivative identity to every exact normalized weak conditional and, on a crossing cell, to the exact finite macrobridge and joint compact–optical logarithm. Then apply the Banach-valued Faà di Bruno formula to the finite composition. This assertion is coefficientwise and uses only finite-volume differentiability; the uniform compact marked bound is supplied later by Theorem A.20. The one-step summed replica–BKAR theorem controls each weak packet, Lemma A.4 controls zero, one and two g marks, and finite source transport controls the external roots. Uniqueness of the lowest common ancestor is the ordinary tree property. Lemma A.12 (Top marked ancestor and complete-packet shell). In a packet tree from Lemma A.11, contract every maximal descendant subtree carrying no distinguished label into its exact parent-normalized decoration, and suppress every maximal unary chain into its exact ordered transfer product. The distin- guished labels are the two curvature roots and the zero, one or two active g marks. They have a unique lowest common ancestor v⊤; let j be its conditional scale. Call v⊤ the top marked ancestor. All vertices strictly above v⊤ are unmarked. The complete coefficient created at v⊤ includes every contracted side decoration with its canonical reported support. Decompose these whole coefficients by their full reported-support shell k using a scalar translation- and reflection-invariant selector. The selected class is therefore a complete BRS/reflection packet with every denominator and normalization contact still inside it. If j ≤ k + C0, the permanent rooted-support row of that whole packet gives C(1 + 2k−j )q e−c2k−j . (208) If j > k + C0, the full support lies inside a fixed enlargement of one scale-j cell and no spatial connector estimate is needed. Proof. Contraction of an unlabelled descendant is its exact conditional value, so it retains its canonical reported support. Suppression of a unary chain is the exact ordered transfer product. After these operations, a tree with at most four distinguished leaves has only a fixed number of essential branching edges, while all arbitrarily long unary portions are already contained in the parity transfer product. The unique lowest common ancestor of all labels is an ordinary tree invariant. If a g derivative lands on a coarser conditional map, its mark is attached at that map and therefore moves v⊤ to that scale. Hence no marked vertex can occur above v⊤. The shell selector is applied only after the full packet at v⊤ has been collected. A remote unmarked side decoration is not cut from its parent and is not asked to carry an external Taylor factor. If exact localization turns its contribution into a smaller-hull contact, that term is already in the finite contact block and is not a shell-k packet. Otherwise its canonical reported support remains part of the whole packet. When that support spans 2k−j scale-j blocks, the strict rooted-support norm and the weak connector theorem give (208), including covariance, hard-core and mixed alternatives. The exact Loc/collect identity of Theorem 5.8 ensures that this dichotomy is made before the stable packet is declared. 100 Theorem A.13 (Complete rooted even-contact contraction). The weak rooted margin pays every nonroot weak vertex by σ < 1, while the finite macrobridge and matching KP expansion pay the finitely many terminal vertices. The one/two-root bounds control a component while its source leaves remain external. These majorants are summable independently of m, so dominated convergence gives the last assertion. The connected two-buffer part of D2 Wm(0) is the disjoint sum of the matching histories and the weak histories indexed by their unique label j ≥ jw. Unique-coarsest-scale allocation Differentiate the exact composition of normalized conditional logarithms with respect to source parameters. Ties are broken lexicographically. Lemma 8.8 (Canonical multiscale allocation). Every connected two-buffer source history is assigned exactly once either to the matching connector or to one weak scale j ≥ jw. Finer decorations attached to its coarsest skeleton have a uniformly summable one/two-root norm. Put dw := CR + m−1 w . 75 For d ≥ dw, X j≥jw Ce−c0| log σ|(d−CR)/ℓj ≤ C′ e−mw(d−CR)/2 . (116) For d < dw the complete history is retained in one fixed enlarged local cluster: it is uniformly bounded for bounded observables and controlled in the local source topology for renormalized fields. Proof. By Corollary 8.7, the lowest common ancestor of the two source buffers assigns every connected tree to exactly one terminal or weak-scale class. This is the first scale in the exact recursive conditional- logarithm formula at which the two source components are joined. The least-label convention removes automorphism multiplicities before estimates are applied. The fixed root algebra sums every finer decoration independently of the number of active nonroot supports. Theorem 8.9 (Uniform physical two-source connector). There are dloc, A0, m > 0, independent of resolution, microscopic cutoff, volume, and boundary conditions, with the following properties. (a) For two centered bounded positive-scale observables supported in BR(z) and BR(z′ ), the complete connected majorant is bounded at every separation by Cm(BR(z), BR(z′ )) ≤ Km(z, z′ ) := A0e−m(|z−z′ |1 −2R)+ , (117) and sup z ℓ4 m X z′ Km(z, z′ ) + sup z′ ℓ4 m X z Km(z, z′ ) ≤ K0. (118) (b) If either buffer carries a fixed finite renormalized local source, displacement mark, or rotation mark, then for test sources with disjoint supports at distance d ≥ dloc the connected functional is bounded by Cs,A,Be−md . At shorter distance it is controlled in the local Schwartz/source seminorm supplied by the triangular finite-jet renormalization; no cutoff-uniform pointwise estimate on the diagonal is claimed. The exponent m is common to both classes and to every fixed finite source family. Proof. Combine Lemma 8.5 with the exact forest allocation and the weak scale sum. For d ≥ dloc choose dloc so the variable x = mw(d − CR)+ in Lemma 8.8 is at least one. Weak histories then decay with rate at least mw/2, while matching histories decay with rate at least 1 2 min{mtr, msp}. Hence one may take the common exponent m = m∗ from (111); Lemma 8.2 shows explicitly that this exponent is not degraded by increasing the ultraviolet depth. For bounded observables, the covariance and every short-distance connected local atom are bounded by their sup norms, so enlarging A0 extends the bound to all separations. Lemma 8.1 and integrability of the physical exponential kernel give (118). For a normalized local source, the short-distance cluster is not estimated pointwise. It is exactly the local source coefficient renormalized by the finite triangular counterterm matrix; the finite triangular source theorem printed in Part V bounds it in a finite Schwartz seminorm. Once two source supports are separated, the lowest-common-ancestor edge lies outside both local clusters and the same connector exponent applies. Thus the theorem derives a cutoff-independent physical rate without suppressing or hiding contact terms. All-order source trees Let κm,n(z1, . . . , zn) = Cum(Om,z1 , . . . , Om,zn ). Lemma 8.10 (Labelled-tree extraction). Every connected source-labelled history admits a deterministic allocation to a spanning tree on {1, . . . , n}. An edge joining bounded positive-scale roots is bounded by Theorem 8.9(a). For renormalized local sources, every contact subtree is first contracted into one local renormalized vertex; an edge joining two disjoint vertices is then bounded by Theorem 8.9(b). Only the two endpoint clusters of one edge are retained as roots, and all previously exposed variables are exterior data. 76 Proof. Start with the least label and expose the connected history until it first reaches the least unseen label. Draw that edge, absorb the exposed component into the parent cluster, and repeat. Every new label is attached once. When an edge is estimated, Theorem 6.44 treats the two endpoint clusters as the only distinguished roots and is uniform in all already exposed exterior variables. Theorem 8.11 (All-order physical source-tree bound). For every fixed finite family of centered bounded positive-scale source types there is Cs < ∞ such that |κm,n(z1, . . . , zn)| ≤ n!Cn s X T ∈Tn Y {i,j}∈E(T ) Km(zi, zj), (119) uniformly in resolution, cutoff, volume, and boundary conditions. For every fixed finite family of renormalized local or marked sources, the corresponding smeared cumulants satisfy |Cum(Φm(f1), . . . , Φm(fn))| ≤ Cn n Y i=1 pN (fi) (120) for one finite Schwartz seminorm pN depending on the source types. If the source labels split into two groups with mutually separated supports, the connected cumulant has the additional common factor e−md . Proof. Differentiate the connected source logarithm and first collect each maximal contact subtree into one local root. Apply Lemma 8.10; Theorem 6.44 shows that no matching-stage derivative of order n is required, because each tree edge sees only its two endpoint clusters as distinguished roots. For bounded roots, every edge has the pointwise connector (117); excess attachments are summed by the unused strict KP margin, and the deterministic allocation costs at most the displayed factorial. For renormalized local sources, first contract every contact subtree into its finite local source vertex. The remaining edges join disjoint clusters and carry the separated-support exponential from Theorem 8.9(b). The local vertices are bounded by the triangular source-renormalization seminorms. Repeated Schur summation of the remaining edges, with one Schwartz L1 norm at the tree root, gives (120) and the separated-group exponential. Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence. If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are bounded by Ce−md with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3, together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11 permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed. In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation; factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition formula. Lemma 8.13 (OS linear-growth condition from the common source ball). For every fixed finite source multiplet there are a finite Schwartz seminorm pN , a radius ρN > 0, and CN < ∞ such that its limiting Schwinger distributions satisfy |Sn(f1, . . . , fn)| ≤ n! Cn N n Y i=1 pN (fi). The constants are compatible under the nested finite source multiplets. In particular the projective Schwinger family satisfies the OS linear-growth condition consumed in SI–5. Proof. Corollary 8.12 supplies one cutoff- and volume-independent analytic ball for the normalized source functional in the Banach norm induced by pN . Apply the one-variable Cauchy estimate to z 7→ W(z P i Ji) and polarize the resulting derivative bound. The common radius gives n!ρ−n N , and the finite incidence constants are absorbed in Cn N . The diagonal limit preserves the inequality, and the nested source construction preserves the constants after enlargement. 77 Lemma 8.14 (Reflection positivity inheritance under exact recombination). Every limiting positive-time quadratic form used in the common Schwinger system is the limit of a reflection-positive quadratic form under the original Wilson measure. Exact normal-chart, coarea, compact-pivot, and source transfor- mations are changes of variables or observable identities, not replacement probability measures, and all regular/irregular and half-cylinder labels are recombined before the OS form is taken. Therefore reflection positivity passes to the common limiting family. Proof. At finite cutoff and aligned reflection volume, let Fi be any finite family of constructed positive-time observables. Pull each Fi back through the exact block, fiber, and source identities to a gauge-invariant function of the original positive-time links. Exact recombination makes this pullback independent of the auxiliary labels. Reflection positivity of the Wilson measure gives X i,j cicj⟨ΘFi Fj⟩a,L ≥ 0. The equality is unchanged by the exact coordinate transformations because all Jacobian and normalization factors remain inside the same normalized integral once. Every entry of the finite matrix converges along the common subsequence, so the closed positive-semidefinite cone contains the limiting matrix. This proves the claim. Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered, permutation-symmetric, reflection-positive, continuously translation-covariant Schwinger distributions for the countable algebra generated by the bounded positive-scale observables and every fixed finite normalized local source family. The distributions are projectively compatible and satisfy uniform physical exponential clustering. Proof. The Schwartz estimate gives equicontinuity on each finite tensor power. Diagonal extraction over a countable dense test/source family gives the common projective system. Permutation symmetry passes as an algebraic identity, while Lemma 8.14 gives the complete reflection-positivity mechanism after exact label recombination. Riemann- sum translation errors vanish by the physical Schur bound, Corollary 8.12 gives clustering, and Lemma 8.13 gives the OS linear-growth row. 8.2 Ward identities and Euclidean covariance Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie algebra g: (I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space; (I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four; (I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection- even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density; (I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block. The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension. Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite- dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric tensor exists only for type Ar, and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions occur. 8.2.1 Global displacement source An explicit gauge-covariant link source Let Uρ(x) denote the link in a signed coordinate direction ρ. Ward identity and contact hierarchy For a compactly supported smooth vector field v, let e0 µν(v) = 1 2 (∂µvν + ∂νvµ) − 1 4 δµν∂· v. The scalar strain is represented separately by the coupling-conjugate insertion Θ(1 4 ∂ · v), and the antisymmetric strain by the rotation action. 85 Theorem 8.34 (Exact continuum translation Ward identity). For every compactly supported smooth v and bounded geometric cylinders A1, . . . , An, W(e0 (v); A1, . . . , An) + Θ  1 4 ∂· v; A1, . . . , An  + A(a(v); A1, . . . , An) = n X i=1 S(A1 · · · δvAi · · · An). (160) The coefficient of every contact is exactly one. Repeated differentiation gives the complete contact hierarchy, including the finite tensor-spin Lie derivative for nonscalar insertions. Proof. At finite cutoff, compact-Haar integration by parts for the globally smooth clover vector field gives the exact score identity. The global clover theorem shows that its action on each bounded geometric representative differs from the anchored geometric displacement by O(ℓ2 j ), uniformly after bounded exterior insertions. The right side therefore converges to the geometric derivatives. Theorem 8.33 passes the score decomposition on the left. Differentiating the source functional repeatedly distributes the derivation among the inserted observables, so all collision contacts are fixed rather than independently chosen. If v(A, B) belongs to the terminal matching logarithm, call the history a matching history. Otherwise its scale label j(v) is the unique weak coarsest-connection scale. Notice that this is a property of the exact recursively generated tree, not a choice made after absolute values or bounds have been applied. Corollary 8.7 (No omission and no double counting). The connected two-buffer part of D2 Wm(0) is the disjoint sum of the matching histories and the weak histories indexed by their unique label j ≥ jw. Contracting all strict descendants of v(A, B) produces a one/two-root decorated vertex whose norm is bounded uniformly by Theorem 7.2 and Corollary 7.4. The decomposition remains exact after regular and irregular sectors are recombined. Every connected tree has exactly one lowest common ancestor for the two source leaves, and the recursive least-label convention gives it exactly one representation in Lemma 8.6. Descendants have strictly finer labels or belong to the same finite conditional vertex and are summed by its connected cumulant. Exact regular/irregular recombination precedes the conditional logarithm at every scale, so the forest formula expands the full measure rather than a conditioned all-good measure. Unique-coarsest-scale allocation Differentiate the exact composition of normalized conditional logarithms with respect to source parameters. Every connected term has a finite collection of local objects carrying scale labels. Contract every maximal connected collection of finer-scale objects into a decorated root and declare the largest physical scale remaining to be the coarsest scale of the history. Ties are broken lexicographically. Lemma 8.8 (Canonical multiscale allocation). Every connected two-buffer source history is assigned exactly once either to the matching connector or to one weak scale j ≥ jw. Finer decorations attached to its coarsest skeleton have a uniformly summable one/two-root norm. Put dw := CR + m−1 w . 75 For d ≥ dw, X j≥jw Ce−c0| log σ|(d−CR)/ℓj ≤ C′ e−mw(d−CR)/2 . (116) For d < dw the complete history is retained in one fixed enlarged local cluster: it is uniformly bounded for bounded observables and controlled in the local source topology for renormalized fields. Proof. By Corollary 8.7, the lowest common ancestor of the two source buffers assigns every connected tree to exactly one terminal or weak-scale class. This is the first scale in the exact recursive conditional- logarithm formula at which the two source components are joined. The least-label convention removes automorphism multiplicities before estimates are applied. The fixed root algebra sums every finer decoration independently of the number of active nonroot supports. Since ℓj = 2−(j−jw) ℓjw , put x = mw(d − CR)+. The scale sum is bounded by C P r≥0 exp(−2r x). The condition d ≥ dw gives x ≥ 1, and then this is at most C1 exp(−x), proving (116). If d < dw the two buffers lie in one fixed enlarged physical neighborhood. Their ultraviolet contact subtrees are not summed as separate long connectors; they are contracted into the exact local atom described in the statement. Theorem 8.9 (Uniform physical two-source connector). There are dloc, A0, m > 0, independent of resolution, microscopic cutoff, volume, and boundary conditions, with the following properties. (a) For two centered bounded positive-scale observables supported in BR(z) and BR(z′ ), the complete connected majorant is bounded at every separation by Cm(BR(z), BR(z′ )) ≤ Km(z, z′ ) := A0e−m(|z−z′ |1 −2R)+ , (117) and sup z ℓ4 m X z′ Km(z, z′ ) + sup z′ ℓ4 m X z Km(z, z′ ) ≤ K0. (118) (b) If either buffer carries a fixed finite renormalized local source, displacement mark, or rotation mark, then for test sources with disjoint supports at distance d ≥ dloc the connected functional is bounded by Cs,A,Be−md . At shorter distance it is controlled in the local Schwartz/source seminorm supplied by the triangular finite-jet renormalization; no cutoff-uniform pointwise estimate on the diagonal is claimed. The exponent m is common to both classes and to every fixed finite source family. Proof. Combine Lemma 8.5 with the exact forest allocation and the weak scale sum. For d ≥ dloc choose dloc so the variable x = mw(d − CR)+ in Lemma 8.8 is at least one. Weak histories then decay with rate at least mw/2, while matching histories decay with rate at least 1 2 min{mtr, msp}. Hence one may take the common exponent m = m∗ from (111); Lemma 8.2 shows explicitly that this exponent is not degraded by increasing the ultraviolet depth. For bounded observables, the covariance and every short-distance connected local atom are bounded by their sup norms, so enlarging A0 extends the bound to all separations. Lemma 8.1 and integrability of the physical exponential kernel give (118). For a normalized local source, the short-distance cluster is not estimated pointwise. It is exactly the local source coefficient renormalized by the finite triangular counterterm matrix; the finite triangular source theorem printed in Part V bounds it in a finite Schwartz seminorm. Once two source supports are separated, the lowest-common-ancestor edge lies outside both local clusters and the same connector exponent applies. Thus the theorem derives a cutoff-independent physical rate without suppressing or hiding contact terms. All-order source trees Let κm,n(z1, . . . , zn) = Cum(Om,z1 , . . . , Om,zn ). Lemma 8.10 (Labelled-tree extraction). Every connected source-labelled history admits a deterministic allocation to a spanning tree on {1, . . . , n}. An edge joining bounded positive-scale roots is bounded by Theorem 8.9(a). For renormalized local sources, every contact subtree is first contracted into one local renormalized vertex; an edge joining two disjoint vertices is then bounded by Theorem 8.9(b). Only the two endpoint clusters of one edge are retained as roots, and all previously exposed variables are exterior data. 76 Proof. Start with the least label and expose the connected history until it first reaches the least unseen label. Draw that edge, absorb the exposed component into the parent cluster, and repeat. Every new label is attached once. When an edge is estimated, Theorem 6.44 treats the two endpoint clusters as the only distinguished roots and is uniform in all already exposed exterior variables. Theorem 8.11 (All-order physical source-tree bound). For every fixed finite family of centered bounded positive-scale source types there is Cs < ∞ such that |κm,n(z1, . . . , zn)| ≤ n!Cn s X T ∈Tn Y {i,j}∈E(T ) Km(zi, zj), (119) uniformly in resolution, cutoff, volume, and boundary conditions. If the source labels split into two groups with mutually separated supports, the connected cumulant has the additional common factor e−md . Proof. Differentiate the connected source logarithm and first collect each maximal contact subtree into one local root. Apply Lemma 8.10; Theorem 6.44 shows that no matching-stage derivative of order n is required, because each tree edge sees only its two endpoint clusters as distinguished roots. For bounded roots, every edge has the pointwise connector (117); excess attachments are summed by the unused strict KP margin, and the deterministic allocation costs at most the displayed factorial. For renormalized local sources, first contract every contact subtree into its finite local source vertex. The remaining edges join disjoint clusters and carry the separated-support exponential from Theorem 8.9(b). The local vertices are bounded by the triangular source-renormalization seminorms. Corollary 8.12 (Uniform source analyticity and exponential clustering). On a fixed neighborhood of the origin, every finite source functional is analytic uniformly in resolution and volume. Thermodynamic and zero-spacing limits of all fixed finite source derivatives exist along one common diagonal subsequence. If two groups of source supports are separated by physical distance d, their connected full moments are bounded by Ce−md with the same m > 0 as in Theorem 8.9. The local source incidence and Lemma 8.3, together with the finite compact matching expansion, give a common analytic source ball. Theorem 8.11 permits termwise differentiation and a countable diagonal extraction. The finite-volume approximants are chosen on a cofinal schedule from Corollary 6.2. The extraction is made once for the countable algebra and a fixed countable set of dyadic physical source scales, and the resulting subsequence is relabelled as the directed sequence appearing in Theorem 1.1; no uniqueness or schedule-independence claim is needed. In every connected partition joining two separated groups, at least one tree edge crosses the separation; factor its exponential and sum all remaining edges with (118). Full moments follow from the set-partition formula. Lemma 8.13 (OS linear-growth condition from the common source ball). Every limiting positive-time quadratic form used in the common Schwinger system is the limit of a reflection-positive quadratic form under the original Wilson measure. Theorem 8.15 (Common positive-scale Schwinger system). The common subsequence defines tempered, permutation-symmetric, reflection-positive, continuously translation-covariant Schwinger distributions for the countable algebra generated by the bounded positive-scale observables and every fixed finite normalized local source family. The distributions are projectively compatible and satisfy uniform physical exponential clustering. Proof. The Schwartz estimate gives equicontinuity on each finite tensor power. Riemann- sum translation errors vanish by the physical Schur bound, Corollary 8.12 gives clustering, and Lemma 8.13 gives the OS linear-growth row. 8.2 Ward identities and Euclidean covariance Theorem 8.16 (Fixed-group invariant-jet admission through dimension six). For a compact simple Lie algebra g: (I1) the invariant symmetric bilinear forms form a one-dimensional space; (I2) there is no reflection-compatible gauge-invariant local vector of engineering dimension at most four; (I3) modulo total differences, equations of motion, and the reflection-odd topological density, the reflection- even scalar dimension-four block is generated by the Yang–Mills density; (I4) the gauge-invariant jet quotient through dimension six is finite. A primitive cubic symmetric color tensor occurs precisely for Lie type Ar with r ≥ 2 and is retained in the dimension-six block; its presence does not enlarge the dimension-four marginal block. The filtered finite-matrix transport and associated-graded contraction used below therefore apply for every fixed G after enlarging only the finite jet dimension. Proof. Simplicity gives the uniqueness of the invariant bilinear form. Semisimplicity makes every finite- dimensional representation traceless on g. Lorentz index counting, gauge covariance, and reflection parity then exclude a single free vector index through dimension four and leave only the quadratic curvature scalar in the even dimension-four sector. Chevalley’s theorem makes the invariant polynomial algebra finitely generated; the degrees are the exponents plus one. Through degree three a primitive symmetric tensor exists only for type Ar, and it first multiplies three curvatures, hence has engineering dimension six. At every fixed engineering cutoff only finitely many covariant derivatives and invariant contractions occur. 8.2.1 Global displacement source An explicit gauge-covariant link source Let Uρ(x) denote the link in a signed coordinate direction ρ. Apply finite-dimensional Haar integration by parts to Xcl and use Proposition 8.21 on the left-hand side. Theorem 8.28 supplies uniform integrability and projective transport on the right. Finite incidence identities The finite clover and incidence formulas satisfy: 1. exact cancellation of the distinguished-link derivative in the two-plaquette clover; 2. exact gauge covariance under arbitrary lattice G gauge transformations; 3. midpoint/reflection cancellation showing an O(a3 ) per-link strip defect; 4. exact signed-hypercubic covariance of the linear incidence stencil; 5. the zero trace of every first nonlinear adjoint diagonal block. These identities, together with the all-stage marked theorem and the terminal joint Haar–optical connector established in Part III, complete the finite-cutoff source construction. 8.2.4 Stress distribution and Ward hierarchy Finite tensor roots and exact transport Let ℓj ↓ 0 be the common directed physical resolutions and let T 0 j,z;µν be the symmetric traceless dimension-four local root obtained from the complete mixing block of Theorem 8.26 in the scale-j cell z. Total differences, the declared equation-of-motion contact convention, the scalar coupling-conjugate root, and the antisymmetric rotation root are kept in separate blocks. For a compactly supported smooth symmetric traceless profile f, set Tj(f) = ℓ4 j X z fµν (z)T 0 j,z;µν. (153) The factor ℓ4 j is the physical Riemann weight. Equation (154) and bound (155) survive every exact weak or macrobridge conditional pushforward. At matching, the stress insertion remains one of the finite local curvature-jet roots in the exact joint law. The complete matching logarithm is differentiated before either compact or optical variables are marginalized, so normalization contacts, mixed retained/optical histories, and the geometric Ward contact remain in the same connected forest. The marked two-root theorem supplies the required local row with a fixed factor, and the exact change-of-variables coefficient of the Ward contact remains one. Projective convergence with bounded exterior sources Let A1, . . . , An be bounded compatible geometric cylinders and use their exact representatives at scale j. Define Wj(f; A1, . . . , An) = Sj Tj(f) n Y i=1 Ai,j ! . (157) Theorem 8.32 (One-stress all-order source-tree bound). There are fixed N, C, a > 0 such that |Wj(f; A1, . . . , An)| ≤ Cn n! pN (f) n Y i=1 ∥Ai∥∞ , (158) uniformly in j, cutoff, volume and boundary conditions. Connected versions obey the same physical spanning-tree decay as the bounded-source theorem, with the stress buffer used as one distinguished root. Proof. Expand the stress-marked connected logarithm into background families and bounded source atoms. Root the canonical labelled exploration at the stress buffer. Each edge of the extracted spanning tree requires at most the stress root and one bounded source buffer, so Corollary 7.4 applies exactly as in the bounded-source tree theorem. Families confined to a fixed buffer are absorbed into the exact local conditional. Every nonlocal attachment pays the physical connector kernel; excess attachments are summed by the unused KP margin. Lemma 8.30 bounds the stress root uniformly and adds no source-count factor. Theorem 8.33 (Projective stress Schwinger distribution). Along the common directed subsequence, the limit W(f; A1, . . . , An) = lim j→∞ Wj(f; A1, . . . , An) (159) exists simultaneously for the countable rational Schwartz/source core and extends uniquely by continuity. It is: (S1) linear and Schwartz tempered in f; (S2) multilinear and continuous in bounded exterior cylinders; (S3) local relative to the common gauge-invariant source system; (S4) continuously translation covariant and signed-hypercubic covariant; (S5) independent of the compatible microscopic representative. Proof. Apply (154) between consecutive resolutions. The exact finite cocycle moves the retained leading jet to the next scale, while the reported high-jet route is part of Rj; Lemma 8.30 bounds the total difference by the summable sequence (156). Thus every matrix element is Cauchy. The uniform tree bound gives a finite Schwartz seminorm and permits diagonal extraction on the countable core. Translation continuity, locality, clustering, and representative independence follow from the uniform rooted source-tree bound and the summable recurrence above. The tensor source is signed-hypercubic at finite cutoff. Its complete filtered low-jet block is normalized by Theorem 8.24; the remaining antisymmetric regulator response is two engineering dimensions higher after one or two geometric marks. The angular Ward theorem below then integrates the resulting infinitesimal action to O(4) covariance. Ward identity and contact hierarchy For a compactly supported smooth vector field v, let e0 µν(v) = 1 2 (∂µvν + ∂νvµ) − 1 4 δµν∂· v. The scalar strain is represented separately by the coupling-conjugate insertion Θ(1 4 ∂ · v), and the antisymmetric strain by the rotation action. 85 Theorem 8.34 (Exact continuum translation Ward identity). For every compactly supported smooth v and bounded geometric cylinders A1, . . . , An, W(e0 (v); A1, . . . , An) + Θ  1 4 ∂· v; A1, . . . , An  + A(a(v); A1, . . . , An) = n X i=1 S(A1 · · · δvAi · · · An). (160) The coefficient of every contact is exactly one. Repeated differentiation gives the complete contact hierarchy, including the finite tensor-spin Lie derivative for nonscalar insertions. Proof. At finite cutoff, compact-Haar integration by parts for the globally smooth clover vector field gives the exact score identity. The global clover theorem shows that its action on each bounded geometric representative differs from the anchored geometric displacement by O(ℓ2 j ), uniformly after bounded exterior insertions. The right side therefore converges to the geometric derivatives. Theorem 8.33 passes the score decomposition on the left. Exact conditional pushforward and the joint matching transport preserve the score identity by Lemma 8.31. Differentiating the source functional repeatedly distributes the derivation among the inserted observables, so all collision contacts are fixed rather than independently chosen. Corollary 8.35 (Distributional conservation away from contacts). For pairwise separated scalar inser- tions, ∂µ * Tµν(x) n Y i=1 Oi(xi) + = − n X i=1 δ(x − xi)∂xν i * n Y i=1 Oi(xi) + , (161) where T denotes the complete tensor distribution consisting of the traceless and scalar strain channels. For tensor insertions, ∂xi is replaced by the full infinitesimal Lie derivative. Proof. Integrate (160) by parts in v and use the arbitrariness of the compactly supported profile. Locality separates the ordinary contact terms from pairwise-disjoint supports. Proposition 8.36 (Noncollapse and canonical normalization). The stress Schwinger distribution is nonzero. Its normalization is fixed uniquely by the unit contact coefficient in (160); no independent finite factor is available. Proof. The reflected curvature source of Theorem 10.5 (proved independently in Section 10) defines a nonzero centered local vector. Common physical clustering gives its time-translation correlation a finite positive spectral measure supported away from zero; hence that correlation is not constant and its distributional translation derivative is nonzero for suitable separated test profiles. Choose v supported near one insertion. The right side of (160) is then nonzero, so the tensor insertion on the left cannot vanish. Multiplying the tensor by a finite constant would multiply the left contact coefficient but not the geometric derivation on the right, forcing the constant to be one. Result and local-field realization Theorem 8.37 (Stress/Ward construction). The global displacement theorem plus the projective construc- tion above produce a nonzero tempered Euclidean tensor insertion on the bounded geometric Schwinger algebra, with exact translation contacts and signed-hypercubic covariance. The later angular Ward theorem promotes its continuum multiplet to O(4) covariance. 8.2.5 Euclidean rotation covariance Low-dimensional rotation-defect classification. Lemma 8.38 (No low-dimensional gauge-invariant vector). For every compact simple G there is no nonzero local, reflection-compatible, gauge-invariant vector source of engineering dimension at most four, modulo vacuum terms and total derivatives. Proof. A connection mass term is not gauge invariant. At dimension two, Tr dρ(Fµν) = 0 by semisimplicity. At dimension three, traces of dρ(DF) reduce to derivatives of Tr dρ(F) and vanish. A dimension-four gauge-invariant vector cannot be formed from two curvature two-forms using only the metric while leaving one free index; an epsilon tensor would have the wrong reflection parity. Total derivatives are excluded by the stated quotient. 86 Lemma 8.39 (Antisymmetric stress defect begins at dimension six). A local signed-hypercubic, reflection- even, gauge-invariant antisymmetric rank-two source has no component of engineering dimension four or five. Its first possible nonzero continuum jet has dimension at least six. Proof. At dimension four the quadratic curvature tensors are generated by Tr(FµαFνα), δµν Tr F2 , and the corresponding dual-curvature contractions. The first is symmetric; the dual contraction is proportional to a diagonal pseudoscalar tensor; none has an antisymmetric part. Reflection symmetry excludes the odd-derivative dimension-five sector. Corollary 8.40 (Vanishing rotation regulator). In the projective stress Ward identity, the antisymmetric regulator insertion is a2 times a normalized family of dimension-six roots. The marked source bounds therefore make it vanish in the zero-spacing limit. The cutoff rotation Ward identities integrate to full O(4) covariance once the already established translation, reflection, and tensor-source continuity statements are inserted. Stress Ward identity and the antisymmetric regulator source For a compactly supported smooth vector field v, write e0 µν(v) = 1 2 (∂µvν + ∂νvµ) − 1 4 δµν∂· v, aµν(v) = 1 2 (∂µvν − ∂νvµ). The global smooth clover displacement theorem and Haar integration by parts give, after projective source transport, T (e0 (v); O) + Θ  1 4 ∂· v; O  + A(a(v); O) = n X i=1 Sn(O1, . . . , δvOi, . . . , On), (162) where O = (O1, . . . , On) is a finite family of local gauge-invariant source fields. The contact coefficient is one, and for tensor fields δv is the full geometric Lie derivative, including the finite spin action. The first two terms in (162) are the symmetric traceless and scalar metric strains. The third is the regulator response to the antisymmetric part of the lattice displacement. Lemma 8.41 (Vanishing antisymmetric-strain source). For every fixed finite source family and compactly supported antisymmetric profile ωµν , the finite-cutoff antisymmetric insertion has the form Aa(ω) = a2 R(6) a (ω), where R (6) a is a finite sum of dimension-six-and-higher marked local roots. (163) Consequently A(ω; O) = 0 in the continuum Ward identity. Proof. Apply the complete filtered normalization of Theorem 8.24 to the rank-two source block. The prescribed principal vector lies in Wst ⊕Wsc and is symmetric in its frame indices. Therefore its contraction with an antisymmetric strain is exactly zero. Any raw hypercubic-only dimension-four coefficient, including the diagonal channel (134), has already been removed through degree D by the complete finite inverse; it is not inferred to vanish from its principal symbol. After this equal-dimensional subtraction, the residual regulator response is the complementary row in Theorem 8.26. Reflection symmetry and centered discretization exclude dimension five, so it is a2 times a dimension-six-and-higher marked root. The arbitrary finite marked source tree supplies the remaining seminorms and proves (163).
